\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\prod_{i}g_{i}X(u_{i})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})\times X^{r}

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\prod_{i}g_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})

Note that for
g=(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
general in
G^{r}
, the scheme
\mathcal{W}_{g}={\mathcal{W}}_{u_{1},\dots,u_{r}}^{g,\mathbf{d}}
is the variety parametrizing genus zero, degree
\mathbf{d}
, stable maps to
X
sending their
i
-th marked point within
g_{i}\cdot X(u_{i})
, and
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{W}}_{u_{1},\dots,u_{r}}^{g,\pi_{*}\mathbf{d}}
is the variety parametrizing genus zero, degree
(\pi_{*})\mathbf{d}
stable maps sending their
i
-th marked point within
g_{i}\cdot X^{\prime}(u_{i})
. Indeed, according to the proof of Kleiman’s transversality theorem
[
Kle74
]
, for a point
g=(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
general in
G^{r}
, the natural maps
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(G/P,{\mathbf{d}})
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(G/P^{\prime},{\mathbf{d}})
are both closed immersions. This allows us to identify the schemes
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
with their images in the space of stable maps.

Proposition 1.2.4.1
.

Suppose the morphism
\Pi\times{\rm ev}
is surjective, and suppose its general fiber is connected.

1.

For
g
general in
G^{r}
,
(\Pi\times ev)(\mathcal{W}_{g})=\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
and
\Pi(\mathcal{W}_{g})=\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
;

2.

For
g
general in
G^{r}
, each irreducible component of the Gromov-Witten variety
\mathcal{W}_{g}
surjects into a different irreducible component of
\Pi(\mathcal{W}_{g})
, i.e. there is a
1
-to-
1
correspondence between irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
and irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
;

3.

Suppose the general fiber of
\Pi\times ev
is rationally connected. Then for
g
general in
G^{r}
, the Gromov-Witten variety
\mathcal{W}_{g}
is a rationally connected fibration over both
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
, in the sense of Definition
1.1.2.1
.

Lemma 1.2.4.1
.

Let
S
be a normal scheme, and
S\xrightarrow{f}Y
be a surjective morphism. If the general fiber of
f
is connected, then each irreducible component of
f(S)
is dominated by exactly one irreducible component of
S
.

Démonstration.

First, observe that, since the general fiber of
f
is connected, two connected components of
S
cannot dominate the same irreducible component of
f(S)
. Indeed, if we consider a dense open subset
U
of
f(S)
such that the fiber
f^{-1}(s)
is connected for all
s
in
U
, two connected components of
S
dominating the same irreducible component of
f(S)
would have a point in common in their image and in
U
; these two connected components hence would be connected within
S
. Moreover, since
S
is normal, its connected components are irreducible. This implies all irreducible components of
f(S)
are dominated by exactly one irreducible component of
S
.
∎

For
u
in
W
, we denote by
x(u):=uP/P
the
T
-fixed point in
X(u)=\overline{BuP/P}
associated with
u
.

Lemma 1.2.4.2
.

Let
u\in W
.

i)

Let
f:Z\rightarrow X(u)
be a
B
-equivariant morphism. Then there exists a surjective map
f^{-1}(BuP/P)\rightarrow f^{-1}(x(u))
.

ii)

Denote by
\pi_{|u}
the restriction of the projection
\pi
to
X(u)
. The general fiber of the surjective morphism
\pi_{|u}:X(u)\rightarrow X^{\prime}(u)
is an irreducible unirational variety.

Démonstration.

This is implied by
[
CMBP13
]
, Proposition 2.3. We include here a proof for completeness. We denote by
U=BuP/P
the open
B
-orbit of the
T
-fixed point
x(u)
.

i)

Denote by
s:U\rightarrow B
a section of
B\rightarrow BuP/P
, i.e. a morphism such that for all elements
x
in
U
,
s(x)\cdot x(u)=x
. Such a section exists according to
[
CMBP13
]
, Proposition 2.2. Note that for
y
in
f^{-1}(U)
, the element
\left(s(f(y))\right)^{-1}\cdot y
lies in
f^{-1}(x(u))
. Indeed, since
f
is
B
-equivariant, we have
f(\left(s(f(y))\right)^{-1}\cdot y)=\left(s(f(y))\right)^{-1}\cdot f(y)=x(u)
. We obtain a morphism :

\displaystyle f^{-1}(U)

\displaystyle\rightarrow f^{-1}(x(u))\times U

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\left(s(f(y))\right)^{-1}\cdot y;f(y))

which is an isomorphism ; indeed, the morphism
(p,u)\rightarrow s(u)\cdot p
defines its inverse. Composition with the first projection induces a surjective morphism
f^{-1}(U)\rightarrow f^{-1}(x(u))
.

ii)

According to
i)
, there exists a surjective map
\pi_{|u}^{-1}(U)\rightarrow\pi_{|u}^{-1}(x(u))
. Hence, since
\pi_{|u}^{-1}(U)
is a dense open subvariety of the irreducible rational variety
X(u)
, the fiber
\pi_{|u}^{-1}(x(u))
is an irreducible unirational variety. Furthermore, since
\pi_{|u}
is
B
-equivariant, the fiber
\pi_{|u}^{-1}(x)
at a point
x
in
U
is isomorphic to
\pi_{|u}^{-1}(x(u))
. Indeed, if
x=b\cdot x(u)
, where
b
lies in
B
, the morphism
y\rightarrow b^{-1}\cdot y
defines an isomorphism between
\pi^{-1}(x)\cap X(u)
and
\pi^{-1}(x(u))\cap X(u)
, since
X(u)
is
B
-stable. Hence the general fiber of
\pi_{|u}
is isomorphic to
\pi_{|u}^{-1}(x(u))
, which is an irreducible unirational variety.

∎

We name
{(\Pi\times ev)}_{|\mathcal{W}_{g}}:\mathcal{W}_{g}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
the restriction of the morphism
\Pi\times ev
to the Gromov-Witten variety
\mathcal{W}_{g}
.

Lemma 1.2.4.3
.

If the general fiber of
\Pi\times ev
satisfies a property
(P)
, then for
g
general in
G^{r}
the general fiber of
(\Pi\times ev)_{|\mathcal{W}_{g}}
also satisfies
(P)
.

Démonstration.

Name
M:=\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,{\mathbf{d}})
. Let
U
be a dense open subset of
(\Pi\times ev)(M)
such that the fiber of
\Pi\times ev
over a point in
U
satisfies
(P)
. Let
(m,x)
be a point in
U
, where
m
lies in
M^{\prime}:=\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}{\mathbf{d}})
and
x
lies in
X^{r}
. Note that, if
(m,x)
is in the image
(\Pi\times ev)({\mathcal{W}}_{g})
, then the fiber over
(m,x)
of
(\Pi\times ev)_{|\mathcal{W}_{g}}
satisfies
(P)
. Indeed, we have :

\displaystyle(\Pi\times ev)_{|\mathcal{W}_{g}}^{-1}(m,x)

\displaystyle=\left(M_{m}\times_{X^{r}}\{x\}\right)\times_{X^{r}}(g\prod_{i}X(u_{i}))

\displaystyle=M_{m}\times_{X^{r}}\left(\{x\}\times_{X^{r}}(g\prod_{i}X(u_{i}))\right)

\displaystyle=M_{m}\times_{X^{r}}\{x\}

\displaystyle=(\Pi\times ev)^{-1}(m,x),

where the third equality holds since
(m,x)
is in
(\Pi\times ev)({\mathcal{W}}_{g})
, and hence
x
lies in
ev({\mathcal{W}}_{g})\subset g\prod_{i}X(u_{i})
. Since
(m,x)
lies in
U
, the fiber
(\Pi\times ev)^{-1}(m,x)
satisfies
(P)
; hence the fiber
(\Pi\times ev)_{|\mathcal{W}_{g}}^{-1}(m,x)
satisfies
(P)
for all
(m,x)
in
(\Pi\times ev)({\mathcal{W}}_{g})\cap U

Finally, note that for
g
general in
G^{r}
, according to Lemma
1.2.3.1

{\mathcal{W}}_{g}\cap(\Pi\times ev)^{-1}(U)
is dense in
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}
. Hence
(\Pi\times ev)({\mathcal{W}}_{g})\cap U
is dense in
(\Pi\times ev)({\mathcal{W}}_{g})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.2.4.1
.

1.

Since the morphism
\Pi\times ev
is surjective, this follows directly from Proposition
1.2.3.1

i)
, applied to
h=\Pi:M=\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow M^{\prime}=\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})
,
f={\rm ev}:\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow Z=X^{r}
,
f^{\prime}={\rm ev}:\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})\rightarrow Z^{\prime}=(X^{\prime})^{r}
, and from considering the integral group
G^{r}
.

2.

We proceed in two steps ; we first prove that each irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
surjects into a different irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
, before proving the correspondence with irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
.

Correspondence between irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
and
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
.
First, note that, since the space
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
and Schubert varieties are normal
[
FP96
,
Ram85
]
, according to Kleiman’s transversality theorem
\mathcal{W}_{g}=ev^{-1}(g_{1}X(u_{1})\times\dots g_{r}X(u_{r}))
is normal for
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
general in
G^{r}
. Furthermore, since the general fiber of
\Pi\times ev
si connected, according to Lemma
1.2.4.3
for
g
general in
G^{r}
the general fiber of the restriction
{(\Pi\times ev)}_{|\mathcal{W}_{g}}
is connected. Hence according to Lemma
1.2.4.1
all irreducible components of
(\Pi\times ev)(\mathcal{W}_{g})
are dominated by exactly one irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
.

We then only have to ensure that there is no irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
whose image is a strict closed subset of an irreducible component of
(\Pi\times ev)(\mathcal{W}_{g})=\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
. Proposition
1.2.3.1

iii)
, tells us that, since
\mathcal{W}_{g}
is in general position, the image by
\Pi
of any of its irreducible components is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
, hence the
1
-to-
1
correspondence.

Correspondence between irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
.
According to Lemma
1.2.4.2
the general fiber of the restriction
\pi_{u}:\prod_{i}X(u_{i})\rightarrow\prod_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})
of
\pi^{r}
to
X(u_{1})\times\dots\times X(u_{r})
is irreducible, hence there exists an open subvariety
U
of
X^{\prime}(u_{1})\times\dots\times X^{\prime}(u_{r})
such that the fiber of
\pi
at
x
in
U
is irreducible. Consider the projections
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
defined by the following Cartesian diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i}g_{i}X(u_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i}g_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ev}

Note that the fiber
p_{1}^{-1}(m)
at a point
m
in
p_{2}^{-1}(g\pi_{u}^{-1}(U))
is irreducible. Indeed, we have :

\displaystyle p_{1}^{-1}(m)

\displaystyle=\{m\}\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\left(g_{1}X(u_{1})\times\dots g_{r}X(u_{r})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\pi_{u}^{-1}(ev(m))

\displaystyle\simeq g\cdot\pi_{u}^{-1}(g^{-1}ev(m)),

which is irreducible since
ev(m)
lies in
g\pi_{u}^{-1}(U)
. Furthermore, according to Lemma
1.2.3.2

b)
, for
g
general in
G^{r}
,
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}\cap p_{2}^{-1}(g\pi_{u}^{-1}(U))
is a dense open subvariety of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}=p_{2}^{-1}(\prod_{i}g_{i}X(u_{i}))
. Hence the general fiber of
p_{1}
is irreducible. Furthermore, note that, since
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})
and Schubert varieties are normal
[
FP96
,
Ram85
]
, according to Kleiman’s transversality theorem

\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}=\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}\mathbf{d})\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\left(g_{1}X(u_{1})\times\dots g_{r}X(u_{r})\right)

is normal for
g=(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
general in
G^{r}
. According to Lemma
1.2.4.1
, all irreducible components of
p_{1}(\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime})=\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
are thus dominated by exactly one irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
.

Finally, Proposition
1.2.3.1

iv)
, tells us that, since
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
is in general position, the image by
p_{1}
of any of its irreducible components is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
, hence the
1
-to-
1
correspondence.

To sum up,
each irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
surjects into a different irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
, and each irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime}
surjects into a different irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{W}_{g}
surjects onto
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
. There thus is a
1
-to-
1
correspondence between irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}
and irreducible components of
\mathcal{W}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
.

3.

The only point left to prove is that the general fiber of
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}\rightarrow{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
is an irreducible rationally connected variety. According to Lemma
1.2.4.2
there exists a dense open subset
U
of
\prod_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})
such that the fiber of
\pi_{|u}:\prod_{i}X(u_{i})\rightarrow\prod_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})
over a point in
U
is an irreducible rationally connected variety. For
g
general in
G^{r}
, the following properties are satisfied :

(a)

\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\right)_{g\cdot U}
is dense in
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}=\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\right)_{g\cdot\prod_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})}
;

(b)

The general fiber of
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
is rationally connected ;

(c)

The general fiber of
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}\rightarrow{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime}
is rationally connected ;

(d)

The general fiber of
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}\rightarrow{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
is rationally connected.

Note that proving
(d)
is our goal here.

(a)
holds according to Lemma
1.2.3.1
. Note that the fiber of the projection
p_{1}:{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}
over a point
m
in
\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\right)_{g\cdot U}
is rationally connected ; indeed, it is isomorphic to
g\cdot(\pi_{|u})^{-1}(g^{-1}\cdot ev(m))
, where
g^{-1}\cdot ev(m)
lies in
U
. Hence, since according to
(a)

\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\right)_{g\cdot U}
is dense in
{\mathcal{W}}_{g}^{\prime\prime}=\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X^{\prime},\pi_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\right)_{g\cdot\prod_{i}X^{\prime}(u_{i})}
,
(b)
holds.
(c)
holds according to Lemma
1.2.4.3
. Finally,
(d)
is deduced from
(b)
,
(c)
and Theorem
1.2.2.1

(3)
.

∎

1.3
Balanced flags of vector bundles

Let
X
be a flag variety. We describe here the flag of vector bundles associated with a general point in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
for a degree
{\mathbf{d}}
high enough. All definitions and results presented in this part are contained in I. Coskun’s description of rational curves on a flag variety
[
Cos10
]
, or easily deduced from it.

1.3.1
Notations.

Fix two positive integers
m<n
. Let
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\}
be a set of integers satisfying
0<i_{1}<\dots<i_{m}<n
. Recall
Fl_{I}
is the
m
-step flag variety parametrizing
m
-tuples
(V_{i_{1}},\dots,V_{i_{m}})
of vector subspaces of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
, where the subspaces are ordered by inclusion
0\subset V_{i_{1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{i_{m}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
, and
V_{i_{k}}
is of dimension
i_{k}
.
The class
\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
of a curve
C
in
X
is determined by non-negative integers
d_{1}
,
d_{2}
,
\dots
,
d_{m}
, where
d_{i}
is the Plücker degree of the projection of
C
to the Grassmannian
{\rm Gr}(n_{i},n)
. A point
p
in
\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X,\mathbf{d})
is associated with a morphism
f_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
. By the functorial definition of flag varieties, the morphism
f_{p}:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\rightarrow X
is uniquely determined by the data of a flag of vector bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
:

\mathcal{F}_{p}=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n},

where
\mathrm{rank}E_{k}=i_{k}
and
\mathrm{deg}E_{k}=-d_{k}
. Note that we have
f_{p}^{*}S_{i}\simeq E_{i}
, where
S_{i}
is the
i
-th tautological vector bundle over
X
.

1.3.2
Admissible sets of sequences.

Recall
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\}
is a set of
m
integers
0<i_{1}<\dots<i_{m}<n
.

Definition 1.3.2.1
.

Consider a set
\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
of
m
non negative integers. A
(I,\mathbf{d})
-admissible set of sequences
is a set

A_{\bullet}=\{(a_{1,j})_{j=1}^{i_{1}},(a_{2,j})_{j=1}^{i_{2}},\dots,(a_{m,j})_{j=1}^{i_{m}}\}

of
m
sequences of non-negative integers of lengths
i_{1}
,
\dots
,
i_{m}
, respectively, such that :

1.

0\leq a_{k,j}\leq a_{k,j+1}
for every
k
and every
1\leq j\leq i_{k}-1
;

2.

a_{k+1,j}\leq a_{k,j}
for every
1\leq k\leq m-1
and
j\leq i_{k}
;

3.

\sum_{j=1}^{i_{k}}a_{k,j}=d_{k}
for every
k
.

Definition 1.3.2.2
.

A
(I,\mathbf{d})
-admissible set of sequences
A_{\bullet}=\{(a_{k,j})\}
is balanced if

A_{\bullet}

minimizes the function

\sum_{1\leq k\leq m}\sum_{1\leq l<p\leq n_{i}}(a_{k,p}-a_{k,l})

among the
(I,\mathbf{d})
-admissible sets of sequences.

Note that, according to
[
Cos10
]
(see the proof of Lemma
2.1
), for any set
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
in
\mathbb{N}^{m}
there exists a unique balanced
(I,{\mathbf{d}})
-admissible set of sequences
(a_{k,j})
, which can be constructed by iteration on
k
in the following way.

\bullet

First, set
a_{1,j}=\lceil d_{1}/n_{1}\rceil-\epsilon_{1,j}
where
\epsilon_{1,j}
is in
\{0,1\}
and is chosen in such a way that
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{1}}a_{1,j}=d_{1}
and
a_{1,j}\leq a_{1,j+1}
and
\lceil q\rceil
denote the ceiling of
q\in\mathbb{Q}
;

\bullet

Let
1<k\leq m
. Suppose all
a_{k-1,j}
have been constructed. Denote by
r_{k}
the largest index such that the element
a_{k-1,r_{k}}
satisfies
a_{k-1,r_{k}}\leq d_{k}/n_{k}
. Set :

a_{k,j}=a_{k-1,j}\>\mathrm{for}\>1\leq j\leq r_{k}.

(1.5)

Denote by
R_{k}
the largest index
>r_{k}
such that the element
a_{k-1,R_{k}}
satisfies
a_{k-1,R_{k}}\leq(d_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{k}}a_{k-1,j})/(i_{k}-r_{k})
. Repeat the process replacing
r_{k}
with
R_{k}
until all elements
a_{k-1,j}
of index larger than
r_{k}
are larger than
(d_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{k}}a_{k-1,j})/(i_{k}-r_{k})
. Then set :

\forall r_{k}<j\leq i_{k},\>a_{k,j}=\left\lceil\frac{(d_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{k}}a_{k-1,j})}{(a_{k}-r_{k})}\right\rceil-\epsilon_{k,j},

(1.6)

where
\epsilon_{k,j}
is in
\{0,1\}
and is chosen in such a way that
\sum_{j=1}^{i_{k}}a_{k,j}=d_{k}
and
a_{k,j}\leq a_{k,j+1}
.

For simplicity, we set
d_{0}:=0
and
n_{0}:=0
.

Proposition 1.3.2.1
.

Let
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
be a set of nonnegative integers. Let
1<k\leq m
such that :

\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq i_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{i_{p}-i_{p-1}}\right\rceil.

(1.7)

Then the balanced
(I,{\mathbf{d}})
-admissible set of sequences
\{(a_{1,j})_{j=1}^{i_{1}},\dots,(a_{m,j})_{j=1}^{i_{m}}\}
satisfies
a_{k,j}=a_{k-1,j}
for
1\leq j\leq i_{k-1}
.

Démonstration.

By iteration on
s<k
, we prove that for
1\leq s<k
all elements
a_{s,j}
satisfy
a_{s,j}\leq d_{k}/n_{k}
. According to (
1.5
) this will yield the expected result. First, for
s=1
, since
d_{k}\geq n_{k}\lceil d_{1}/i_{1}\rceil
,
a_{1,j}\leq d_{k}/i_{k}
. Now let
1\leq s<k
, and suppose
a_{s-1,j}\leq d_{k}/i_{k}
for all
1\leq j\leq n_{s-1}
. Note that, if
r_{s}<i_{s-1}
, then there exists elements
a_{s-1,j}
larger than
(d_{s}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{s}}a_{s-1,j})/(i_{s}-r_{s})
, where we use the notations of (
) ; hence all elements
a_{s,j}
are smaller than an element
a_{s-1,s^{\prime}}
for some
s^{\prime}
. Hence all elements
a_{s,j}
then satisfy
a_{s,j}\leq d_{k}/n_{k}
. On the other hand, if
r_{s}=i_{s-1}
, then according to (
) :

\displaystyle\forall i_{s-1}<j\leq i_{s},\>\>\>\>a_{s,j}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lceil\frac{\left(d_{s}-\sum_{j=1}^{i_{s-1}}a_{s-1,j}\right)}{(i_{s}-i_{s-1})}\right\rceil

\displaystyle\leq\left\lceil\frac{(d_{s}-d_{s-1})}{(i_{s}-i_{s-1})}\right\rceil\>\>\>\>\leq\frac{d_{k}}{n_{k}}.

∎

1.3.3
Balanced flag bundles.

The partial flag variety
Fl_{I}
comes equipped with
m
tautological bundles
S_{1}
,
\dots
,
S_{m}
. We describe here the pullback
f_{p}^{*}S_{i}
by a morphism
f_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
associated with a point
p
general in
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X,\mathbf{d})}
.

Let
\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
be an effective class of curve in
H_{2}(Fl_{I},\mathbb{Z})
,
p
be a point in
{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
, and
f_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
be its associated morphism. The pullback
f_{p}^{*}S_{s}
of the
s
-th tautological bundle
S_{s}
by
f_{p}
is a vector bundle of degree
d_{s}
and rank
i_{s}
. According to Grothendieck’s decomposition of vector bundles,
f_{p}^{*}S_{s}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of line bundles

f_{p}^{*}S_{s}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{s,1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{s,i_{s}}),

where
\sum_{j=1}^{i_{s}-k_{s}}a_{s,j}=d_{s}
.
Suppose, for a given
s
, the
a_{s,j}
are ordered in increasing order. Then, since, for all
1<s\leq m
,
f_{p}^{*}S_{s-1}
is a subbundle of
f_{p}^{*}S_{s}
, we have :
a_{s-1,j}\geq a_{s,j}
. Hence the sequence
(a_{s,j})
forms a
(I,\mathbf{d})
-admissible set of sequence in the sense of Definition
1.3.2.1
. We call
a_{s,j}
the
admissible set of sequences associated with
p
.

Proposition 1.3.3.1
.

i)

The admissible set of sequences associated with a general point in
\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(Fl_{I},\mathbf{d})
is balanced in the sense of Definition
1.3.2.2
;

ii)

Suppose
\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq n_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{i_{p}-i_{p-1}}\right\rceil
. Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}=E_{1}\hookrightarrow\dots\hookrightarrow E_{m}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
the flag of vector bundles associated with a general element
{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},\mathbf{d})
. Then
E_{k}
splits into
E_{k}\simeq E_{k-1}\oplus_{\sum_{s=n_{k-1}+1}^{i_{k}}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-a_{k,s})
.

Démonstration.

i)

Cf. proof of
[
Cos10
]
, Proposition
2.3.

ii)

Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
be the flag of vector bundles associated with a general element
{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
. Then, according to
i)
, the admissible set of sequences associated with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is balanced. Since
d_{k}
satisfies
\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq i_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{i_{p}-i_{p-1}}\right\rceil
, we have according to Proposition
1.3.2.1
the following sequence :

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow

\displaystyle E_{k-1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k-1,1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k-1,n_{k-1}})\rightarrow

\displaystyle\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>E_{k}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k-1,1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k-1,i_{k-1}})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k,i_{k-1}+1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k,n_{k}})

which splits. Indeed, for all
j
,
l
verifying
j\leq l
and
j\leq n_{k-1}<l
, we have :
a_{k,j}=a_{k-1,j}
and
a_{k,j}\leq a_{k,l}
, hence
a_{k-1,j}\leq a_{k,l}
for any
l>i_{k-1}
.
Hence according to Lemma
1.3.3.1
the morphisms
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k-1,j})\rightarrow E_{k}
factor through
\oplus_{b=1}^{n_{k-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k,b})\hookrightarrow E_{k}
. Finally, since the morphism
E_{k-1}\rightarrow E_{k}
is injective,
E_{k-1}
is a rank
i_{k-1}
subvector bundle of
\oplus_{b=1}^{i_{k-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k,b})
; hence
E_{k-1}\simeq\oplus_{b=1}^{n_{k-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{k,b})
.

∎

Lemma 1.3.3.1
.

Let
d_{1}\leq\dots\leq d_{r}
and
d_{1}^{\prime}=d_{1}\leq\dots\leq d_{r}^{\prime}=d_{r}\leq d_{r+1}^{\prime}\leq\dots\leq d_{r+p}^{\prime}
be sequences of non negative integers. Then the following sequence splits.

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow

\displaystyle E=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{r})\rightarrow

\displaystyle\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>F=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{r})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{r+1}^{\prime})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{r+p}^{\prime})

Démonstration.

Let
k
be the largest index such that
d_{k}<d_{r}
. Note that for
i>k
all the integers
d_{i}^{\prime}
are greater than
d_{k+1}
or equal to
d_{k+1}
and
d_{k+1}=d_{r}>d_{k}
. Hence all integers
d_{i}^{\prime}
are greater than
d_{k}
for
i>k
. Hence the induced morphism
E_{1}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{k})\rightarrow F
factors through a closed immersion
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{k})\hookrightarrow F
. Denote by
r^{\prime}
the largest index such that
d_{r^{\prime}}^{\prime}=d_{r}
. Note that
r^{\prime}\geq r
. Let us now consider the induced exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow

\displaystyle E/E_{1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{k+1})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{r})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{k})^{\oplus r-k}

\displaystyle\>\>\>\>\rightarrow F/E_{1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{k+1}^{\prime})\oplus\dots\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-d_{r+p}^{\prime})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{r})^{\oplus(r^{\prime}-k)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{r^{\prime}+1})\oplus\dots\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{r+p}^{\prime}).

Since for any
i>r^{\prime}
the integer
d_{i}^{\prime}
is greater than
d_{k}
, the injective morphism
E/E_{1}\rightarrow F/E_{1}
factors through

E/E_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{k})^{\oplus r-k}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{r})^{\oplus(r^{\prime}-k)}\hookrightarrow F/E_{1}

Finally observe that the morphism
E/E_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{k})^{\oplus r-k}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{r})^{\oplus(r^{\prime}-k)}
factors through
E/E_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{k})^{\oplus r-k}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{k})^{\oplus r-k}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d_{k})^{\oplus r^{\prime}-r}\hookrightarrow F/E_{1}
.
∎

1.4
Forgetting one vector space

We denote by
\pi_{\widehat{k}}:X\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
the morphism forgetting the
k
-th vector space. Let
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
be a degree in
E(X)
. In this section, our goal is to prove the following results.

Proposition 1.4.0.1
.

Suppose
\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq n_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{n_{p}-n_{p-1}}\right\rceil
.
Then the general fiber of

\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,{\mathbf{d}})\rightarrow\left(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev\right)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,{\mathbf{d}}))\subset\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(X_{\widehat{k}})^{r}}X^{r}

is a unirational variety.

Corollary 1.4.0.1
.

Suppose
\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq n_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{n_{p}-n_{p-1}}\right\rceil
and the map
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev
is surjective. Then for
x
general in
X^{r}
, the general fiber of the map
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,{\mathbf{d}})\ni ev^{-1}(x)\to ev^{-1}(\pi_{\widehat{k}}(x))\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})
is a unirational variety.

We will assume in this section
\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq n_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{n_{p}-n_{p-1}}\right\rceil
. Let
(p,x)
be a point in
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm ev})({{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
, where
p
is in
{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})*\mathbf{d})
, and
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{r})
is in
X^{r}
. Denote by
\Pi_{p,x}
the fiber of
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm ev}
at
(p,x)
, i.e. :

\Pi_{p,x}:=\Pi_{\widehat{k}}^{-1}(p)\times_{X^{r}}x.

In order to prove the irreducibility and unirationality of
\Pi_{p,x}
, we define an auxilliary variety
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
, which dominates
\Pi_{p,x}
when
(p,x)
is general in the image
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)({{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
.

1.4.1
Notations.

The class
\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
of a curve
C
in
X
is determined by non-negative integers
d_{1}
,
d_{2}
,
\dots
,
d_{m}
, where
d_{i}
is the Plücker degree of the projection of
C
to the Grassmannian
{\rm Gr}(n_{i},n)
. The morphism
\pi_{\widehat{k}}:X\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
induces a morphism
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}\mathbf{d})
.

Denote by
\pi_{k}:X\rightarrow{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
the morphism sending a
m
-step flag
\{0\}\subset V_{1}\subset\dots\subset V_{k}\subset\dots\subset V_{m}
to the
k
-th linear subspace
V_{k}
. This morphism induces a morphism
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}({\rm Gr}(n_{k},n),d_{k})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}({\rm Gr}(n_{k},n),d_{k})
into the space of genus zero stable maps with three marked points to the Grassmannian
{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
.
We obtain the following commutative diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X,\mathbf{d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}({\rm Gr}(n_{k},n),d_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}\mathbf{d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\widehat{k}}}^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\widehat{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\widehat{k}}\times\dots\times\pi_{\widehat{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}\times\dots\times\pi_{k}}

Furthermore, for
p
in
\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X,\mathbf{d})
, we will denote by :

f_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X

the associated morphism. By the functorial definition of flag varieties,
f_{p}
is uniquely determined by the data of a flag of vector bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
:

\mathcal{F}_{p}=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n},

where
\mathrm{rank}E_{i}=n_{i}
and
\mathrm{deg}E_{i}=-d_{i}
. Note that we have
f_{p}^{*}S_{i}\simeq E_{i}
, where
S_{i}
is the
i
-th tautological vector bundle over
X
. Finally, for a point
{\mathfrak{p}}
in
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
we will denote by
(E_{i})_{\mathfrak{p}}
the fiber at
{\mathfrak{p}}
of the vector bundle
E_{i}
, which has a natural inclusion
(E_{i})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
induced by the morphism
E_{i}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
.

1.4.2
A preliminary result.

Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{p}=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{k-1}\subset E_{k+1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
the flag of vector bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
associated with the point
p
in
\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}\mathbf{d})
.

Lemma 1.4.2.1
.

i)

Consider a dense open subset
U
of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
. Then, for
(p,x)
general in
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
,
\Pi_{p,x}\cap U
is a dense open subset of
\Pi_{p,x}
;

ii)

For
(p,x)
general in
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
, there exists a dense open subset
U
of
\Pi_{p,x}
such that, for all
q
in
U
, the flag of vector bundles
\mathcal{F}_{q}
associated with
q
is balanced.
Hence there exists an increasing set of integers
a_{n_{k-1}+1}\leq\dots\leq a_{n_{k}}
such that for all
q
in
U
, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{q}
is of the form :

\mathcal{F}_{q}=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{k-1}\subset E_{k}\simeq E_{k-1}\oplus_{i=n_{k-1}+1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i})\subset E_{k+1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}.

Démonstration.

i)

Since
(p,x)
is general in
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
, according to Lemma
1.2.2.1
the intersection of
\Pi_{p,x}
with the dense open subset
U
is dense in
\Pi_{p,x}
.

ii)

According to Proposition
1.3.3.1

i)
, there exists a dense open subset
U
of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
such that a point in
U
corresponds to a balanced flag of vector bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. According to
i)
,
U\cap\Pi_{p,x}
is dense open subset of
\Pi_{p,x}
.
Finally, notice that according to Proposition
1.3.3.1

ii)
, since
\forall\>p<k,\>d_{k}\geq n_{k}\left\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{n_{p}-n_{p-1}}\right\rceil
, the flag of vector bundles associated with a point in
\Pi_{p,x}\cap U
splits at
E_{k-1}
.

∎

1.4.3
Definition of
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
.

Our goal here is to construct a rational variety
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
dominating
\Pi_{p,x}
. First, consider the integers
a_{n_{k-1}+1}
,
\dots
,
a_{n_{k}}
defined in Lemma
1.4.2.1

ii)
and the following vector space

V:=Hom(\oplus_{i=n_{k-1}+1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}).

The flag variety
X
is naturally embedded in
X_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
by Plücker-embedding
X_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
in a product of projective spaces, and cutting out
X
from this product by considering the relations given by the inclusions of flags. Note that, if we denote by
[f_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}},\{p_{1},\dots,p_{r}\}]
the stable map associated with
p
, points
q
in
\Pi_{p,x}\cap\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X,\mathbf{d})
correspond to stable maps
[f_{q}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X,\{p_{1},\dots,p_{r}\}]
satisfying
f_{q}(p_{i})=x_{i}
and such that the following diagram is commutative :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\widehat{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{p}}

Furthermore, by the functorial definition of the Grassmannian, elements
u
in
V
corresponding to an injective morphism

\Phi_{u}:E_{k-1}\oplus_{i}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}

define a morphism
f_{u}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
, where we denote by
\Phi_{u}
the morphism induced by the inclusion
E_{k-1}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
and the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
associated with a point
u
in
V
. Conversally, any injective morphism
E_{k-1}\oplus_{i}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
can, up to automorphisms of
E_{k-1}
, be obtained this way. Points in
\Pi_{p,x}
are thus associated with elements
u
in
V
such that :

1.

\Phi_{u}:E_{k-1}\oplus_{i=n_{k-1}+1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
is an injective morphism of vector bundles ;

2.

For all
1\leq i\leq r
,
({F_{u}})_{p_{i}}=\pi_{k}(x_{i})
;

3.

f_{p}\times f_{u}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
factors through
X\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
,

where we denote by
({F_{u}})_{p_{i}}
the fiber of the vector bundle
F_{u}:=\Phi_{u}(E_{k-1}\oplus_{i}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i}))\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
at the point
p_{i}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, and by
f_{u}
the morphism associated with the flag of vector bundles
F_{u}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
by functorial definition of the Grassmannian.
Note that for
u
in
V
the morphism
\Phi_{u}:E_{k-1}\oplus_{i}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-a_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
might not be an injective morphism, and the fiber
(F_{u})_{p_{i}}
might be of dimension less than
n_{k}
. In order to define a closed subset of
V
, we define
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
as the subset of elements
u
in
V
such that :

\displaystyle\forall\>i\in\>\{1,\dots,r\},\>(F_{u})_{p_{i}}\subset\pi_{{k}}(x_{i}),\>\mathrm{where}\>\pi_{{k}}(x_{i})\in{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n);

(1.8)

\displaystyle\forall\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbb{P}^{1},\>(F_{u})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset({E_{k+1}})_{\mathfrak{p}},

(1.9)

where we denote by
\pi_{k}(x_{i})
the vector subspace of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
of dimension
n_{k}
associated with the point
\pi_{k}(x_{i})
in
{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
, and by
({E_{k+1}})_{\mathfrak{p}}
the fiber of the vector bundle
E_{k+1}
at
\mathfrak{p}
. Note that, when
\Phi_{u}
defines a morphism
\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
, Equations (
) and (
) are equivalent to Equations
2.
and
3.
here above. Indeed, by construction of
\Phi_{u}
,
(F_{u})_{\mathfrak{p}}
satisfies
(E_{k-1})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset(F_{u})_{\mathfrak{p}}
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, hence if
u
satisfies Equation (
),
u
also verifies :

\forall{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{P}^{1},\>(E_{1})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset\dots\subset(E_{k-1})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset(F_{u})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset(E_{k+1})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset\dots\subset(E_{m})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n},

hence
\Phi_{u}
defines a morphism to the flag variety
X
.

1.4.4
Irreducibility and rationality of
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
.

We denote by
F_{\mu}
the subsheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus r}
associated with an element
\mu
in
V\simeq Hom(E_{k-1}\oplus_{i}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-a_{i}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus r})
, and by
(F_{\mu})_{\mathfrak{p}}
the fiber over a point
{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
of
F_{\mu}
. We consider the usual construction of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
by gluing two affine schemes together. Let
U_{1}:={\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C}[s])
,
U_{2}:={\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C}[t])
,
U_{12}:={\rm Spec}({\mathbb{C}}[s,s^{-1}])=U_{1}\setminus\{0\}
, and
U_{21}:={\rm Spec}({\mathbb{C}}[t,t^{-1}])=U_{2}\setminus\{0\}
. Then
\mathbb{P}^{1}
can be obtained by gluing
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
together along
U_{12}\simeq U_{21}
, where we consider the following isomorphism :

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}[s,s^{-1}]\simeq{\mathbb{C}}[t,t^{-1}],\>\>\>s\rightarrow t^{-1}.

For
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we view
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d)
as the line bundle satisfying
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d)_{|U_{i}}\simeq U_{i}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
for
i=1,2
, obtained by identifying
U_{12}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}={\rm Spec}[s,s^{-1}]\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
and
U_{21}={\rm Spec}[t,t^{-1}]\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
via the following isomorphism :

(s,v)\rightarrow(s^{-1},s^{d}v).

Equivalently we endow
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-d)
with two trivializations on
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
respectively. We consider here the natural bijection
V\simeq\oplus_{j}({\mathbb{C}}_{a_{j}}[x])^{n}
, associating to a section in
({\mathbb{C}}_{a_{j}}[x])^{n}
the morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-a_{j})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
described by :

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Spec}{\mathbb{C}}[s]\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n}

\displaystyle(s,v)\rightarrow(s,P_{j}(s)v)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Spec}{\mathbb{C}}[s^{-1}]\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n}

\displaystyle(s^{-1},v)\rightarrow(s^{-1},s^{-a_{j}}P_{j}(s)v)

We may hence view a point
\mu
in
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
as a set of
n_{k}-n_{k-1}
vectors
P_{j}
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{C}}_{a_{j}}[x]
. In order to simplify notations, we keep the dependence of the polynomials
P_{j}
on the point
\mu
in
V
associated with them implicit.

Lemma 1.4.4.1
.

1.

There exist
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
p_{1}
,
\dots
,
p_{N}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
, such that
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
is the subspace of elements
\mu
in
V
satisfying the following equations :

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,\dots,r\},\>(F_{\mu})_{p_{i}}\subset\pi_{{k}}(x_{i}),\>\mathrm{where}\>\pi_{{k}}(x_{i})\in{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n);

\displaystyle\forall\iota\in\{1,\dots,N\},\>(F_{\mu})_{p_{\iota}}\subset(E_{k+1})_{p_{\iota}};

2.

Consider a point
\mathfrak{p}
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We denote by
V((E_{k+1})_{\mathfrak{p}})
the subset of elements
\mu
in
V
satisfying
(E_{k+1})_{\mathfrak{p}}\supset(F_{\mu})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
Then
V((E_{k+1})_{\mathfrak{p}})
is a linear subspace of
V
;

3.

\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
is a linear subspace of
V
.

Démonstration.

1.

By definition, the vectors
P_{j}
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{C}}_{a_{j}}[x]
associated with
\mu\in V
satisfy :

\forall{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1},\>(F_{\mu})_{\mathfrak{p}}\simeq\langle(E_{k-1})_{\mathfrak{p}},(P_{n_{k-1}+1}({\mathfrak{p}}),\dots,P_{n_{k}}({\mathfrak{p}}))\rangle,

where we denote by
F_{\mu}
the subvector bundle of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus r}
associated with
\mu
. Denote by
Q_{j}
the
n_{k+1}
homogeneous polynomials associated with the inclusion of vector bundles
E_{k+1}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus r}
. Then there exist
N>0
, and points
p_{1}
,
\dots
,
p_{N}
in
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
such that the following statements are equivalent :

\displaystyle\forall\>{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{P}^{1},\>(F_{\mu})_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset({E_{k+1}})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\>{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{P}^{1},\>\langle P_{n_{k-1}+1}({\mathfrak{p}}),\dots,P_{n_{k}}({\mathfrak{p}})\rangle\subset\langle Q_{1}({\mathfrak{p}}),\dots,Q_{n_{k+1}}({\mathfrak{p}})\rangle

(1.10)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\forall\iota\in\{1,\dots,N\},\>\langle P_{n_{k-1}+1}(p_{\iota}),\dots,P_{n_{k}}(p_{\iota})\rangle\subset\langle Q_{1}(p_{\iota}),\dots,Q_{n_{k+1}}(p_{\iota})\rangle

Indeed,
\mu
satisfies the homogeneous equations (
) iff the polynomials
P_{j}
associated with
\mu
satisfy (
) at
N
different values of
p_{\iota}=[x_{\iota},y_{\iota}]
for
N
large enough. Notice
N
only depends on the degrees
d_{k}
and
d_{k+1}
, which are fixed here.

From now on, we fix such an
N
, and fix points
(p_{r+1},\dots,p_{N})
general in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Equation (
) is thus equivalent to :

\forall\iota\in\{1,\dots,N\},\>(F_{\mu})_{p_{\iota}}\subset({E_{k+1}})_{p_{i}}

(1.11)

2.

We set
{\mathfrak{p}}:=[x:y]
. We will denote by
\langle E,F\rangle
the vector subspace of
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
generated by any two vector subspaces
E
and
F
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
.
The vector subspace
(E_{k+1})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is described by linear equations. Hence :

\displaystyle V((E_{k+1})_{{\mathfrak{p}}})

\displaystyle=\left\{\mu=(P_{i,j})\in V\mid\langle(E_{k-1})_{{\mathfrak{p}}},(F_{\mu})_{p}\rangle\subset(E_{k+1})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\mu=(P_{i,j})\in V\mid\langle(E_{k-1})_{{\mathfrak{p}}},P_{n_{k-1}+1}({\mathfrak{p}}),\dots,P_{n_{k}}({\mathfrak{p}})\rangle\subset(E_{k+1})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\right\}

is the intersection of linear equations on the coefficients of
P_{j}
,
n_{k-1}+1\leq j\leq n_{k}
.

3.

Fix
i
in
\{1\dots,N\}
. Denote by
\pi_{\widehat{k}}(x_{i})
the image of
x_{i}
in the projected flag variety
X_{\widehat{k}}
.
The subset
V_{p_{i}}
of elements
\mu
in
V
satisfying
(F_{\mu})_{p_{i}}\subset\pi_{{k}}(x_{i})
can be described by :

\displaystyle V_{p_{i}}:

\displaystyle=\left\{\mu=(P_{i,j})\in V\mid\langle(F_{\mu})_{p_{i}}\rangle\subset\pi_{k}(x_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\mu=(P_{i,j})\in V\mid\langle P_{n_{k-1}+1}(p_{i}),\dots,P_{n_{k}}(p_{i})\rangle\subset\pi_{k}(x_{i})\right\}.

Since the vector space
\pi_{k}(x_{i})
is defined by linear equations,
V_{p_{i}}
is defined by linear equations on the coefficients of the polynomials
(P_{i,j})
;
V_{p_{i}}
is a linear subspace of
V
.

Finally, according to
1.
the variety
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
is the intersection of the linear spaces
V_{p_{i}}
, for
1\leq i\leq r
, with the linear spaces
V((E_{k+1})_{p_{\iota}})
, for
r<\iota\leq N
.
Hence
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
is a linear subspace of
V
.

∎

According to Lemma
1.4.4.1
,
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
is a linear subspace of
V
, and hence is an irreducible rational variety.

1.4.5
Image of
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X,\mathbf{d})
.

First, denote by
V^{inj}
the subset of elements of
V
corresponding to injective morphisms
E_{k-1}\oplus_{i}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-a_{i})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus r}
. The complementary of
V^{inj}
is described by a set of polynomial equations. Indeed, for
u=(P_{j})_{n_{k-1}+1\leq j\leq n_{k}}
in
V
the associated morphism is injective iff
\langle(E_{k-1})_{\mathfrak{p}},P_{n_{k-1}+1}({\mathfrak{p}}),\dots,P_{n_{k}}({\mathfrak{p}})\rangle
is a vector space of dimension
n_{k}
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. Hence
V^{inj}
is an open subset of
V
. Call

\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}:=\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}\cap V^{inj}.

By the functorial definition of flag varieties, each element
u
in
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}
defines a degree
{\mathbf{d}}
morphism
f_{u}:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\rightarrow X
, which by construction of
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
is associated to a point in
\Pi_{p,x}
. Furthermore, by construction of
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
, any point
q
in
\Pi_{p,x}
whose associated flag of vector bundles is balanced is the image
(F_{u})
of an element
u
in
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}
.
Hence, according to Lemma
1.4.2.1

ii)
, for
(p,x)
general in
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
, the variety
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}
dominates
\Pi_{p,x}
.

1.4.6
Algebraicity of
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}\rightarrow\Pi_{p,x}
.

Denote by
\psi:\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
the function sending an element
(z,{\mathfrak{p}})
in
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
to the point of
X
associated with
(F_{z})_{\mathfrak{p}}
. According to Lemma
1.6.1.1
(Appendix A),
\psi
defines a morphism
\Psi:\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})
, which dominates
\Pi_{p,x}
according to here above.

1.4.7
Proof of Proposition
1.4.0.1
.

Let
(p,x)
be a general point in
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))
. We have obtained a dominating morphism
\Psi:\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}\rightarrow\Pi_{p,x}
. Since
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}
is a dense open subset of the irreducible rational variety
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{p,x}^{inj}
is an irreducible rational variety.
Hence
\Pi_{p,x}
is an irreducible unirational variety.

1.4.8
Proof of Corollary
1.4.0.1
.

For an element
x
in
X^{r}
, we denote by
\Pi_{x}
the restriction of the forgetful map
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,{\mathbf{d}})\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})
to
ev^{-1}(x)
. First, note that since the map
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev
is surjective, according to Proposition
1.2.4.1

1.
for
x
general in
X^{r}
we have
\Pi(ev^{-1}(x))=ev^{-1}(\pi_{\widehat{k}}(x))
. Hence for
x
general in
X^{r}
, the map
\Pi_{x}:ev^{-1}(x)\to ev^{-1}(\pi_{\widehat{k}}(x))
is surjective.

According to Proposition
1.4.0.1
, there exists a dense open subset
U
of
(\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times ev)(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d}))=\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{{X_{\widehat{k}}}^{r}}X^{r}
such that for all elements
(m,x)
in
U
, the fiber
(\Pi\times ev)^{-1}(m,x)=\Pi^{-1}(m)\cap ev^{-1}(x)
is a unirational variety. Denote by
p_{1}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{{X_{\widehat{k}}}^{r}}X^{r}\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})
the first projection and by
p_{2}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},(\pi_{\widehat{k}})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{{X_{\widehat{k}}}^{r}}X^{r}\to X^{r}
the second projection. Note that for all
m
in
p_{1}(p_{2}^{-1}(x)\cap U)
the fiber
\Pi_{x}^{-1}(m)=\Pi^{-1}(m)\cap ev^{-1}(x)=(\Pi\times ev)^{-1}(m,x)
is a unirational variety. Furthermore, note that for
x
general in
X^{r}
,
p_{2}^{-1}(x)\cap U
is a dense open subset of
p_{2}^{-1}(x)
, and hence
p_{1}(p_{2}^{-1}(x)\cap U)
is a dense open subset of
p_{1}(p_{2}^{-1}(x))=\Pi(ev^{-1}(x))
. Hence for
x
general in
X^{r}
the general fiber of
\Pi_{x}
is a unirational variety.

1.5
Proof of Theorem
1.1.2.1
.

Let
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{M}\}
be a set of
M
integers satisfying
0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{M}<n
such that
I
is contained in
J
, let
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{M})
be an element in
\mathbb{N}^{M}
and
1\leq\mu\leq M
be an integer such that the collection
\{I,J,{\mathbf{d}}\}
is stabilized with respect to
\mu
in the sense of Definition
1.1.1.1
.
Note that according to Proposition
1.2.4.1
it is enough to prove that the general fiber of

\displaystyle\Pi_{I/J}\times ev:

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}

(1.12)

is rationally connected. We prove this in two times. We first prove that the general fiber of the morphism
\Pi_{J_{\geq\mu}/J}\times ev
is rationally connected, where
\Pi_{J_{\geq\mu}/J}
is the morphism induced by forgetting vector spaces with indices left of
\mu
not associated with elements in
I
. We then prove that the general fiber of the morphism
\Pi_{I/J_{\geq\mu}}\times ev
is rationally connected. We finally deduce Theorem
1.1.2.1
from these two facts.

1.5.1
Preliminary results on the base change of
\Pi_{I/J}\times ev
.

Let
K=\{k_{1},\dots,k_{m^{\prime}}\}
be a set of integers satisfying
0<k_{1}<\dots<k_{m^{\prime}}
such that
K
is contained in
J
and contains
I
. Our goal here is to prove that the base change of the morphism
\Pi_{I/J}\times ev
defined by (
1.12
) induced by
Fl_{K}\rightarrow Fl_{J}
is a morphism of equidimensional projective varieties, whose general fiber satisfies the same properties as the general fiber of
\Pi_{I/J}\times ev
. Since the projection morphism
Fl_{K}\rightarrow Fl_{I}
factors through
Fl_{J}\rightarrow Fl_{I}
, we are considering the following morphism :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J})^{r}}(Fl_{K})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}\right)\times_{(Fl_{J})^{r}}(Fl_{K})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{K})^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\huge\sim}

First, note that
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J})^{r}}(Fl_{K})^{r}
is a reduced scheme of pure dimension the expected dimension. Indeed, according to Kleiman’s transversality theorem for
g
general in
({\rm SL}_{n})^{r}
the projective scheme
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J})^{r}}g\cdot(Fl_{K})^{r}
is an equidimensional reduced scheme. Furthermore, since the morphism
(Fl_{J})^{r}\rightarrow(Fl_{I})^{r}
is
G:=(SL_{n})^{r}
-equivariant, the following diagram is commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}\left(G\times(Fl_{J})^{r}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\huge\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\huge\sim}

where
p
and
\pi
are the obvious projections. This induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle p^{-1}(g)=\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}\left(g\cdot(Fl_{J})^{r}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\pi^{-1}(g)

\displaystyle\simeq g\cdot\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}

which allows us to deduce the equidimensionality and reducibility of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J})^{r}}(Fl_{K})^{r}
from the equidimensionality and reducibility of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J})^{r}}g\cdot(Fl_{K})^{r}
.

Finally, Lemma
1.5.1.1
here under ensures that the general fiber of the flat base change of
\Pi_{I/J}\times ev
satisfies the same properties as the general fiber of
\Pi_{I/J}\times ev
.

Lemma 1.5.1.1
.

Let
S
be a scheme, let
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
,
M\rightarrow S
and
M^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be schemes over
S
, and
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
be a morphism of schemes over
S
.
Suppose the general fiber of
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
satisfies a property
(P)
, and
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
is flat. Then the general fiber of
M_{S^{\prime}}\rightarrow M^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}
satisfies
(P)
.

Démonstration.

If the morphism
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
is not surjective, it factors through
M\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}M
and then
\mathrm{Im}(M_{S^{\prime}})\simeq(\mathrm{Im}M)_{S^{\prime}}
; hence we can assume
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
surjective. Denote by
U
the dense open subset of
M^{\prime}
such that the fiber of
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
over
\{m\}
in
U
satisfies
(P)
. Note that, since
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
is a flat morphism,
U\times_{S}S^{\prime}
is a dense subset of
M^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}
. Indeed, if we denote by
p_{1}:M^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}\rightarrow M^{\prime}
the first projection, acccording to
[
Gro
]
Proposition 2.3.4, all irreducible components of
p_{1}^{-1}(M^{\prime})
dominate
M^{\prime}
; hence the image of each irreducible component of
M^{\prime}
by
p_{1}
has a non empty intersection with
U
, hence each irreducible component of
M^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}
has a non empty intersection with
U_{S^{\prime}}
. Then for
(m,s)
in
U\times_{S}S^{\prime}\subset M^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}
, we have :

\displaystyle(M\times_{S}S^{\prime})_{(m,s)}

\displaystyle=M\times_{S}\{s\}\times_{M^{\prime}}\{m\}

\displaystyle=(M\times_{M^{\prime}}\{m\})\times_{S}\{s\}\simeq M_{m},

where
M_{m}
satisfies
(P)
since
m
lies in
U
. Hence the general fiber of
M_{S^{\prime}}\rightarrow M^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}
satisfies
(P)
.
∎

1.5.2
Forgetting vector spaces left of
V_{j_{\mu}}
.

Recall a point in
Fl_{J}
corresponds to a flag of vector spaces :

\{0\}\subset V_{j_{1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{j_{M}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

Denote by
\mu^{\prime}
the integer such that
i_{\mu^{\prime}}=j_{\mu}
. A point in
Fl_{J_{\geq\mu}}
corresponds to a flag of vector spaces :

\{0\}\subset V_{i_{1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{i_{\mu^{\prime}}}=V_{j_{\mu}}\subset V_{j_{\mu+1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{j_{M}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

For
1\leq k<\mu
, we name
X_{k}:=(Fl_{J_{\geq k}})^{r}
,
{\mathbf{d}}^{k}:=(\pi_{J_{\geq k}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}}
and
M_{k}:=\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{k},{\mathbf{d}}^{k})
. Forgetting all vector spaces left of
V_{j_{\mu}}
that are not of the type
V_{i_{k}}
induces a morphism
\Pi_{J_{\geq\mu}/J}\times ev:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\rightarrow M_{\mu}\times_{X_{\mu}}(Fl_{J})^{r}
. We consider here the general fiber of this map.

Proposition 1.5.2.1
.

Let
\{I,J,{\mathbf{d}}\}
be a stabilized collection.

i)

The general fiber of the map
\Pi_{J_{\geq\mu}/J}\times ev
is a unirational variety.

ii)

For
x
general in
(Fl_{J})^{r}
, the fiber
ev^{-1}(x)\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})
is a tower of unirational fibrations over the fiber
ev^{-1}(\pi_{J_{\geq\mu}}(x))\in M_{\mu}
.

Démonstration.

We proceed by iteration on
k
.

i)

Let
1\leq k<\mu
such that the general fiber of
\Pi_{J_{\geq k/J}}\times ev:M_{J}\rightarrow M_{k}\times_{X_{k}}(Fl_{J})^{r}
is rationally connected. Suppose
j_{k+1}
is not contained in
I
. Recall

{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{M}).

We denote by

(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}}=(d^{\prime}_{1},\dots,d^{\prime}_{m}).

Denote by
k^{\prime}
the largest integers such that
i_{k^{\prime}}<j_{k}
. Note that

—

The degree
{\mathbf{d}}^{k}
is given by the set

{\mathbf{d}}^{k}=(d_{1}^{\prime},\dots,d_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},d_{k^{\prime\prime}}^{k},\dots,d_{M^{\prime}}^{k}),

obtained from
{\mathbf{d}}
by removing all integers
d_{s}
such that
s<\mu
and there exists no integer
s^{\prime}
satisfying
i_{s^{\prime}}=j_{s}
.

—

The degree
{\mathbf{d}}^{k+1}
is given by the sequence

{\mathbf{d}}^{k+1}=(d_{1}^{\prime},\dots,d_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},d_{k^{\prime\prime}+1}^{k}\dots d_{M^{\prime}}^{k}),

which is obtained from the set
{\mathbf{d}}^{k}
by forgetting the integer
d_{k^{\prime\prime}}^{k}
.

Note that according to Definition
1.1.1.1
for all
p<k
,

d^{k}_{k^{\prime\prime}}\geq\lceil(d^{\prime}_{p}-d^{\prime}_{p-1})/(i_{p}-i_{p-1})\rceil j_{k}.

Then according to Proposition
1.4.0.1
the general fiber of

\Pi_{J_{\geq k+1}/J_{\geq k}}\times ev:M_{k}\rightarrow(\Pi_{J_{\geq k+1}/J_{\geq k}}\times ev)(M_{k})=M_{k+1}\times_{X_{k+1}}X_{k}

is unirational, and hence rationally connected. Furthermore, according to Definition
1.1.1.1
the morphism surjects onto
M_{k+1}\times_{X_{k+1}}X_{k}
. Then according to Subsection
1.5.1
the general fiber of the following morphism

\displaystyle M_{k}\times_{X_{k}}(Fl_{J})^{r}\rightarrow M_{k+1}\times_{X_{k+1}}(Fl_{J})^{r}

is also rationally connected.
Hence according to Theorem
1.2.2.1
by composition the general fiber of
\Pi_{J_{\geq k+1}/J}\times ev:M_{J}\rightarrow M_{k+1}\times_{X_{k+1}}(Fl_{J})^{r}
is rationally connected. In the same way, if
j_{k+1}
is contained in
I
, the general fiber of
\Pi_{J_{\geq k+2}/J}\times ev:M_{J}\rightarrow M_{k+2}\times_{X_{k+2}}(Fl_{J})^{r}
is rationally connected.

ii)

Let
1\leq k<\mu
such that for
x
general in
(Fl_{J})^{r}
, the fiber
ev^{-1}(x)\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})
is a tower of unirational fibrations over the fiber
ev^{-1}(\pi_{J_{\geq k}/J}(x))\in M_{k}
is unirational. Suppose
j_{k+1}
is not contained in
I
. Corollary
1.4.0.1
and Proposition
1.2.4.1
imply that for
x
general in
(Fl_{J})^{r}
, the fiber
ev^{-1}(x)\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})
is a tower of unirational fibrations over the fiber
ev^{-1}((\pi_{J_{\geq k+1}/J}(x))\in M_{k+1}
.

∎

1.5.3
Forgetting vector spaces right of
V_{j_{\mu}}
.

We deduce here the rational connectedness of the general fiber of the morphism
\Pi_{I/J_{\geq\mu}}\times ev
from Subsection
1.5.2
by duality. For a set
K=\{k_{1},\dots,k_{m}\}
of
m
integers satisfying
0<k_{1}<\dots<k_{m}<n
, we call
dual partition
of
K
the set

K^{*}:=\{n-k_{m},\dots,n-k_{1}\}.

Consider the natural transformation of functors sending a flag
\mathcal{F}_{S}:=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus n}
of vector bundles over a scheme
S
to the dual flag
\mathcal{F}_{S}^{*}:=({\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus n}/E_{m})^{*}\subset\dots\subset({\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus n}/E_{1})^{*}\subset({\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus n})^{*}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus n}
, where we have chosen an isomorphism
({\mathbb{C}}^{n})^{*}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
. This natural transformation of functors induces an isomorphism
Fl_{K}\simeq Fl_{K^{*}}
, and we will call
Fl_{K^{*}}
the flag variety
dual to

Fl_{K}
. Note that this induces here an isomorphism between the flag variety
Fl_{J\geq\mu}
parametrizing flags of vector spaces

V_{i_{1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{i_{\mu^{\prime}}}\subset V_{j_{\mu+1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{j_{M}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

and the dual flag variety
Fl_{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}
parametrizing flags of vector spaces

V_{n-j_{M}}\subset\dots\subset V_{n-j_{\mu+1}}\subset V_{n-i_{\mu^{\prime}}}\subset\dots\subset V_{n-i_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

This isomorphism sends a curve of class
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},d_{2}\dots,d_{m})
in
E(Fl_{K})\simeq\mathbb{N}^{m}
onto a curve of class
{\mathbf{d}}^{*}=(d_{m},\dots,d_{2},d_{1})
in
E(Fl_{K^{*}})\simeq\mathbb{N}^{m}
. Furthermore, note that, since
\{I,J,{\mathbf{d}}\}
is stabilized with respect to
\mu
, according to Lemma
1.5.3.1
the collection
\left\{I,(J_{\geq\mu})^{*},\left(\left(\pi_{I/J_{\geq\mu}}\right)_{*}{\mathbf{d}}\right)^{*}\right\}
is stabilized with respect to
n
in the sense of Definition
1.1.1.1
. Hence, according to Subsection
1.5.2
here above, the general fiber of the morphism
\Pi_{I^{*}/(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}\times ev
is rationally connected. Finally, the following commutative diagram allows us to deduce the rational connectedness of the general fiber of
\Pi_{I/J_{\geq\mu}}\times ev
from the rational connectedness of the general fiber of the morphism
\Pi_{I^{*}/(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}\times ev
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{\geq\mu}},(\pi_{{J_{\geq\mu}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\underset{(Fl_{I})^{r}}{\times}(Fl_{J_{\geq\mu}})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}},((\pi_{{J_{\geq\mu}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I^{*}},((\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})\underset{(Fl_{I^{*}})^{r}}{\times}(Fl_{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}})^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{I/J_{\geq\mu}}\times ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{I^{*}/(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}\times ev}

Note that the isomorphism
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{\geq\mu}},(\pi_{{J_{\geq\mu}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\simeq\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}},((\pi_{{J_{\geq\mu}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})
is induced by the isomorphism
Fl_{J\geq\mu}\simeq Fl_{(J\geq\mu)^{*}}
and the isomorphism

\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\underset{(Fl_{I})^{r}}{\times}(Fl_{J_{\geq\mu}})^{r}\simeq\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I^{*}},((\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})\underset{(Fl_{I^{*}})^{r}}{\times}(Fl_{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}})^{r}

is induced by the isomorphisms
Fl_{I}\simeq Fl_{I^{*}}
and
Fl_{J\geq\mu}\simeq Fl_{(J\geq\mu)^{*}}
.

Lemma 1.5.3.1
.

Let
J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{M}\}
be a set of
M
integers satisfying
0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{M}<n
and let
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\}
be a subset of
J
. Let
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{M})
be an element in
\mathbb{N}^{M}
and let
1\leq\mu\leq M
such that the collection
\{I,J,{\mathbf{d}}\}
is stabilized with respect to
\mu
. Then the collection
\left\{I^{*},(J_{\geq\mu})^{*},\left((\pi_{J\geq\mu/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}}\right)^{*}\right\}
is stabilized with respect to
n
.

Démonstration.

We number by
I^{*}:=\{i_{1}^{*},\dots,i_{m}^{*}\}
and
J^{*}=\{j_{1}^{*},\dots,j_{M}^{*}\}
the dual partitions of
I
and
J
, where for any
1\leq k\leq m
,
i_{k}^{*}=n-i_{m-k+1}
and for any
1\leq k\leq M
,
j_{k}^{*}=n-j_{M-k+1}
. Recall
Fl_{I^{*}}
is the flag variety parametrizing flags of vector spaces

V_{n-i_{m}}\subset\dots\subset V_{n-i_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}

and
Fl_{(J\geq\mu)^{*}}
is the flag variety parametrizing flags of vector spaces

V_{n-j_{M}}\subset\dots\subset V_{n-j_{\mu+1}}\subset V_{n-i_{\mu^{\prime}}}\subset\dots\subset V_{n-i_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

We call
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}:=(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}}=(d^{\prime}_{1},\dots,d^{\prime}_{m})
. We call
{\mathbf{d}}^{*}=(d_{1}^{*},\dots,d_{M}^{*})
and
{{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}^{*}=({{d^{\prime}}^{*}}_{1},\dots,{{d^{\prime}}^{*}}_{m})
the dual classes, where for
1\leq k\leq M
,
d_{k}^{*}=d_{M-k+1}
and for
1\leq k\leq m
,
{d^{\prime}}^{*}_{k}=d^{\prime}_{m-k+1}
.
Let
\mu\leq k\leq M
such that
j_{M-k+1}^{*}=n-j_{k}
is not contained in
I^{*}
, i.e. let
\mu<k\leq M
such that
j_{k}
is not contained in the set
I^{*}=I
. Since the collection
\{I,J,{\mathbf{d}}\}
is stabilized with respect to
\mu
, the following properties are satisfied.

—

Let
k^{\prime}
be the smallest integer such that
i_{k^{\prime}}>j_{k}
. Note that
m-k^{\prime}+1
is the largest integer such that
i^{*}_{m-k^{\prime}+1}=n-i_{k^{\prime}}<j^{*}_{M-k+1}=n-j_{k}
. Then for any
k^{\prime}<p\leq m
, i.e. for any
1\leq m-p+1\leq m-k^{\prime}+1
, we have

d^{*}_{m-k+1}=d_{k}\geq\lceil(d^{\prime}_{p}-d^{\prime}_{p+1})/(i_{p+1}-i_{p})\rceil(n-j_{k})=\lceil({d^{\prime}}^{*}_{m-p+1}-{d^{\prime}}^{*}_{m-p})/(i^{*}_{m-p+1}-i^{*}_{m-p})\rceil j^{*}_{M-k+1}.

—

Since the morphism
\Pi_{J_{\mu\leq k}}\times ev
is surjective, the morphism
\Pi_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}}\times ev
is surjective.

Note that the surjectivity of
\Pi_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}}\times ev
is implied by the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k}},(\pi_{{J_{\mu\leq k}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k-1}},(\pi_{{J_{\mu\leq k-1}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\underset{(Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k-1}})^{r}}{\times}(Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k-1}})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(Fl_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}},((\pi_{J_{\mu\leq k}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(Fl_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+2}},((\pi_{J_{\mu\leq k-1}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})\underset{(Fl_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+2}})^{r}}{\times}(Fl_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}})^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{J_{\mu\leq k}}\times ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}}\times ev}

where the isomorphism
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k}},(\pi_{{J_{\mu\leq k}}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\simeq\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,r}}(Fl_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}},((\pi_{J_{\mu\leq k}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})^{*})
is induced by the isomorphism
Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k}}\simeq(Fl_{J_{\mu\leq k}})^{*}=Fl_{{(J_{\geq\mu})^{*}}_{\geq M-k+1}}
.
Hence the collection
\left\{I^{*},(J_{\geq\mu})^{*},\left((\pi_{J\geq\mu/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}}\right)^{*}\right\}
is stabilized with respect to
n
.
∎

1.5.4
Proof of Theorem
1.1.2.1
.

Let
J_{1}:=J_{\geq(\mu;n_{\mu})}
. Consider the morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{1}},(\pi_{J_{1}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J_{1}})_{*}(\pi_{J_{1}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}\right)\times_{(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Huge\sim}

where the first arrow is the morphism obtained by flat base change of
\Pi_{I/J_{1}}\times ev
over
(Fl_{J})^{r}\rightarrow(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}
. According to Subsection
1.5.3
the general fiber of
\Pi_{I/J_{1}}\times ev
is rationally connected, hence according to Lemma
1.5.1.1
the general fiber of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{1}},(\pi_{J_{1}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}
is rationally connected.
Furthermore, according to Subsection
1.5.2
the general fiber of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{1}},(\pi_{J_{1}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}
is also rationally connected. Theorem
1.1.2.1

ii)
then follows from the following commutative diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J},{\mathbf{d}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{J_{1}},(\pi_{J_{1}/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{J_{1}})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(Fl_{I},(\pi_{I/J})_{*}{\mathbf{d}})\times_{(Fl_{I})^{r}}(Fl_{J})^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{J_{1}/J}\times ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{I/J}\times ev}

Indeed, according to Theorem
1.2.2.1
the composition of morphisms of projective complex schemes whose general fiber is rationally connected is a morphism whose general fiber is also rationally connected.

In the same way, since the composition of unirational fibrations is a unirational fibration, Theorem
1.1.2.1

ii)
is easily deduced from Subsections
1.5.3
and
1.5.2
.

1.6
On the surjectivity of
\Pi_{I/J}
.

Let us recall our notations.
We consider the flag variety
X
parametrizing flags
V_{n_{1}}\subset\dots\subset V_{n_{m}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
, where
V_{n_{i}}
is a vector subspace of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
of dimension
n_{i}
. For
1\leq k\leq m
, we denote by
X_{\widehat{k}}
the flag variety obtained from
X
by forgetting the
k
-th vector space, and call
\pi_{\widehat{k}}:X\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
the forgetful morphism.
Let
\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})
be an element in
E(X)\simeq\mathbb{N}^{m}
. We denote by
\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{k-1},d_{k+1},\dots,d_{m})
its pushforward to
E(X_{\widehat{k}})
. In order to ease notations, we set
d_{0}=0=d_{m+1}
. Finally, we denote by
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})
the morphism induced by
\pi_{\widehat{k}}:X\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
.

Our goal here is to give a condition on the degree
\mathbf{d}
, under which the morphism
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})\times_{{X_{\widehat{k}}}^{r}}X^{r}
is surjective. We deduce from it examples of stabilized collections in Subsection
1.6.1
.

Lemma 1.6.0.1
.

Consider an element
p
in general position in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})
. Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{p}=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{k-1}\subset E_{k+1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
the flag of vector bundles associated with
p
. Suppose for all
p<k
,
d_{k+1}\geq n_{k+1}\lceil\frac{d_{p}-d_{p-1}}{n_{p}-n_{p-1}}\rceil
.
Then :

i)

E_{k+1}=E_{k-1}\oplus E_{k+1}/E_{k-1}
;

ii)

E_{k+1}/E_{k-1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}}{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}\rfloor)^{\oplus n-r_{2}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}}{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}\rfloor+1)^{\oplus r_{2}})
, where
r_{2}
is the integer defined by
r_{2}=(d_{k+1}-d_{k-1})-(n_{k+1}-n_{k-1})\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}}{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}\rfloor
;

iii)

There exists a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-vector bundle
F
of degree
-d
where
d\leq d_{k-1}+(n_{k}-n_{k-1})(\left\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}}{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}\right\rfloor+1)
and of rank
n_{k}
such that :

E_{k-1}\subset F\subset E_{k+1}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}.

Démonstration.

i)

Cf. Proposition
1.3.3.1
.

ii)

Cf. the description of balanced flags of vector bundles in Section
1.3.3
.

iii)

Call
d:=d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}
and
n:=n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}
. Consider two non negative integers
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
such that
p_{1}<n-r_{2}
,
p_{2}<r_{2}
, and
p_{1}+p_{2}=n_{k}-n_{k-1}
.

Denote by
F_{0}
the rank
n_{k}-n_{k-1}
subbundle of
E_{k+1}
associated with
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor)^{\oplus p_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor+1)^{\oplus p_{2}})
by the isomorphism
E_{k+1}/E_{k-1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor)^{\oplus n-r_{2}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor+1)^{\oplus r_{2}})
of
ii)
.

Then
F:=E_{k-1}\oplus F_{0}
is a vector bundle of rank
n_{k}
, of degree the opposite of

d_{k-1}+p_{1}(\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor)+p_{2}(\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor+1)\leq d_{k-1}+(n_{k}-n_{k-1})(\lfloor\frac{d}{n}\rfloor+1).

Furthermore,
F
satisfies
E_{k-1}\subset F\subset E_{k+1}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}.

∎

Proposition 1.6.0.1
.

i)

Suppose
d_{k-1}\leq\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}}{n_{k+1}}\rfloor
and
d_{k}\geq d_{k-1}+(n_{k}-n_{k-1})(\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}}{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}\rfloor+1)
.

Then the morphism

\Pi_{\widehat{k}}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})

is surjective ;

ii)

Consider an integer
\tau
such that, for all
d_{k}\geq\tau
, the morphism
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})
is surjective.

Suppose
d_{k}\geq{\tau}+r(n_{k}-n_{k-1})
.
Then the morphism

\Pi_{\widehat{k}}\times{\rm ev}^{r}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})\times_{{X_{\widehat{k}}}^{r}}X^{r}

is surjective.

Démonstration.

i)

Call
\mathbf{d}_{\widehat{k}}:=\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d}
. Consider an element
p
in general position in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\mathbf{d}_{\widehat{k}})
. Since
X
is convex,
\mathcal{M}_{0,3}(X_{\widehat{k}},\mathbf{d}_{\widehat{k}})
is a dense open subset in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\mathbf{d}_{\widehat{k}})
; hence, according to Lemma
1.2.2.1
,
p
is in
\mathcal{M}_{0,3}(X_{\widehat{k}},\mathbf{d}_{\widehat{k}})
. We will construct an antecedent of
p
by
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}
as a concatenation of two morphisms
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
and
g:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
.

Construction of
f
.
Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{p}:=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{k-1}\subset E_{k+1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
the flag of vector bundles associated with
p
. According to Lemma
1.6.0.1

iii)
, there exists a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-vector bundle
F
of degree
d\leq d_{k-1}+(n_{k}-n_{k-1})(\lfloor\frac{d_{k+1}-d_{k-1}}{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}\rfloor+1)
and of rank
n_{k}
defining the following flag of vector bundles :

\mathcal{F}:=E_{1}\subset\dots\subset E_{k-1}\subset F\subset E_{k+1}\subset\dots\subset E_{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}.

By the functorial definition of flag varieties,
\mathcal{F}
defines a degree
(d_{1},\dots,d_{k-1},d,d_{k+1},\dots,d_{m})
morphism
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
, such that
f_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
, factors through
f
. We have denoted by
f_{p}
the morphism associated with
p
.

Construction of
g
.
Denote by
g:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
a rational curve of class
(d_{k}-d)\sigma_{k}
, where
\sigma_{k}
is the degree one Schubert class whose pushforward to
{\rm Gr}(n_{k},n)
is not
0
. Up to multiplication by an element in
{\rm SL}_{n}
, we can assume
g(\mathbb{P}^{1})
intersects
f(\mathbb{P}^{1})
on a point that is not marked. Notice the image of
\pi_{\widehat{k}}\circ g:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
is a point.

Finally,
the stable map
\mathbb{P}^{1}\cup\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
induced by
f
and
g
defines an element in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X,\mathbf{d})
, whose projection by
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}
is
p
.

ii)

Call
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:=\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})
. Fix a genus zero stable map
p:=(g_{p}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\{p_{1},\dots,p_{r}\})
corresponding to a general point in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
. According to Lemma
1.2.3.1
, for any dense open subset
U
of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, the equidimensional variety
X^{r}\times_{(X_{\widehat{k}})^{r}}U
is a dense open subset of
X^{r}\times_{(X_{\widehat{k}})^{r}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
; hence it is enough to prove that
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}^{-1}(\nu)
dominates
X^{r}\times_{(X_{\widehat{k}})^{r}}\{p\}
for a general point
p
in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.

Fix an element
(x,p)
in
X^{r}\times_{(X_{\widehat{k}})^{r}}\{p\}
, i.e. fix
r
points
x_{1}
,
\dots
,
x_{r}
on
X
such that each point
x_{i}
gets projected by
\pi_{\widehat{k}}:X\rightarrow X_{\widehat{k}}
to
g_{p}(p_{i})
. Our goal is to construct an antecedent of
(x,p)
by
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.

We proceed in three steps. First, notice there is a degree
(\mathbf{d}-r(n_{k}-n_{k-1})\sigma_{k})
morphism
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
getting projected to
p
, i.e. such that
g_{p}=\pi_{\widehat{k}}\circ f
. Furthermore, we construct rational curves
C_{i}\rightarrow X
of degree
n_{k}-n_{k-1}
joining the points
f(p_{i})
and
x_{i}
. Finally, by concatenation, this gives a degree
\mathbf{d}
stable map
[\mathbb{P}^{1}\cup_{i}C_{i}\rightarrow X,\{p_{1},\dots,p_{r}\}]
which is an antecedent of
(x,g)
, as researched.

Existence of
f
.
We call :

\boldsymbol{\delta}:=(d_{1},\dots,d_{k-1},d_{k}-r(n_{k}-n_{k-1}),d_{k+1},\dots,d_{m})\in A_{1}(X).

Notice that, since
g
is in general position in
\mathcal{M}_{0,3}(X,\mathbf{d})
and the morphism
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}:\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X,\boldsymbol{\delta})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X_{\widehat{k}},\pi_{\widehat{k}*}\mathbf{d})
is surjective, its reciprocal image
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}^{-1}(p)
has a non empty intersection with the dense open subset
{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}}(X,\boldsymbol{\delta})
of
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\boldsymbol{\delta})
. We can thus find an element
[f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X,\{p_{1},\dots,p_{r}\}]
in
\Pi_{\widehat{k}}^{-1}(\nu)\cap\mathcal{M}_{0,r}(X,\boldsymbol{\delta})
.

Construction of
C_{i}\rightarrow X
.
Fix an integer
i
in
\{1,\dots,r\}
. Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{p_{i}}=\{\ W_{1}^{i}\dots\subset W_{m}^{i}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}\}
the germ at
p_{i}
of the flag of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-vector bundles associated with
f
. Since
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
projects to
g_{p}
, the flag of vector spaces associated with
g_{p}(p_{i})
is :

g_{p}(p_{i})=\{W_{1}^{i}\subset\dots\subset W_{k-1}^{i}\subset W_{k+1}^{i}\subset\dots\subset W_{m}^{i}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}\}.

Denote by
<u_{n_{k-1}+1},\dots,u_{n_{k}}>
a basis of
W_{k}^{i}/W_{k-1}^{i}
.

Recall
x_{i}
is a point in
X
getting projected to
g_{p}(p_{i})
, i.e. corresponds to a flag of vector spaces

W_{1}^{i}\subset\dots\subset W_{k-1}^{i}\subset V\subset W_{k+1}^{i}\subset\dots\subset W_{m}^{i}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}.

Since
{\rm ev}:\overline{\mathcal{M}_{0,2}}({\rm Gr}(n_{k}-n_{k-1},n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}),d)\rightarrow{\rm Gr}(n_{k}-n_{k-1},n_{k+1}-n_{k-1})^{2}
is surjective for
d\geq(n_{k}-n_{k-1})
(cf. for example
[
BM11
]
Corollary 2.2), there exists a degree
(n_{k}-n_{k-1})
genus zero stable map

h_{i}:C_{i}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm Gr}(n_{k}-n_{k-1},n_{k+1}-n_{k-1})

joining the rank
(n_{k}-n_{k-1})
vector spaces
V/W_{k-1}^{i}
and
W_{k}^{i}/W_{k-1}^{i}
within
W_{k+1}^{i}/W_{k-1}^{i}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}
.

By the functorial definition of Grassmannians, each morphism
{h_{i}}_{|{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm Gr}(n_{k}-n_{k-1},n_{k+1}-n_{k-1})
defines a flag of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-vector bundles

E_{j}^{i}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}},

which has one fiber given by the flag of vector spaces
V_{j}/W_{k-1}^{i}\subset W_{k+1}^{i}/W_{k-1}^{i}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}
, and another fiber given by
V_{j+1}/W_{k-1}^{i}\subset W_{k+1}^{i}/W_{k-1}^{i}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{k+1}-n_{k-1}}
, where
V_{1}=V
and
V_{N}=W_{k}
. Now consider the following flag of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-vector bundles :

\mathcal{F}_{i,j}:=W_{1}^{i}\subset\dots\subset W_{k-1}^{i}\subset W_{k-1}^{i}\oplus E_{j}^{i}\subset W_{k+1}^{i}\subset\dots\subset W_{m}^{i}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus n}.

\mathcal{F}_{i,j}
defines a morphism
f_{i,j}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
which is projected to
g_{p}(p_{i})
. Furthermore, the morphisms
f_{i,j}
define a degree
(n_{k}-n_{k-1})
morphism from the tree of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s
C_{i}
to
X
which joins by construction
x_{i}
and
g_{p}(p_{i})
. We will denote by
f_{i}:C_{i}\rightarrow X
the morphism thus obtained.

Construction of an antecedent of
p
.
Finally, consider the stable map
q=[\mathbb{P}^{1}\cup_{i}C_{i}\rightarrow X,\{p_{1},\dots,p_{r}\}]
associated with
[f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X]
and
f_{i}:C_{i}\rightarrow X
, where we place each of the marked points
p_{i}
on an antecedent
f_{i}^{-1}(x_{i})
on
C_{i}
. Since each of the map
f_{i}:C_{i}\rightarrow X
projects to
g_{p}(p_{i})
in
X_{\widehat{k}}
, the morphism
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}(X,\mathbf{d})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
collapses each component
C_{i}
and sends
q
to
p
.

∎

1.6.1
Examples of stabilized collections.

1.

Forgetting the first vector space.
Suppose
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\}
is a collection of integers satisfying
1<i_{1}<\dots<i_{m}<n
and
J=\{j_{1},i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\}
satisfies
0<j_{1}<i_{1}
. Note that then
Fl_{J}\rightarrow Fl_{I}
is the morphism forgetting the first vector space. By definition, if the following condition is satisfied, the collection
\{I,J,{\mathbf{d}}\}
is stabilized with respect to
\mu=2
.

—

The morphism
