. We deduce

\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w_{i,j}}\rangle_{l_{2}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l r}\delta_{i,n-1}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{if}\>p=n\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{if}\>p<n\end{array}\right.

If
k=1
, we have

\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{l_{2}}=0

If
k=2
we have

\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{l_{2}}=\delta_{i,1}\delta_{j,2}.

If
k>2
we have

\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{l_{2}}=\delta_{i,k-1}\delta_{j,1}.

Recall from Lemma
3.6.4.1
that for any
1\leq s,t\leq n
,
s\neq t
,
\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j},{\mathcal{I}}_{s,t}\rangle_{l_{1}}=\delta_{s,n}\delta_{t,j}(1-\delta_{j,n})+\delta_{j,n}\delta_{s,n-1}\delta_{t,n}
. We deduce

\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{2}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w_{i,j}}\rangle_{l_{1}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l r}0&\mathrm{if}\>k=1\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,2}&\mathrm{if}\>k=2\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{if}\>k>2\end{array}\right.

According to Proposition
3.4.1.1
we have

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=0\>\>

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>k=1

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=\delta_{i,k-1}\delta_{j,p}

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>k\neq p+1,\>k\neq 1

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=\delta_{i,k-2}\delta_{j,p}+\delta_{i,k-1}\delta_{j,p+1}-\delta_{i,k-2}\delta_{j,p+1}

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>k=p+1.

Furthermore, according to Lemma
3.6.4.1
for any
1\leq i_{2},j_{2}\leq n
,
i_{2}\neq j_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i_{1},j_{1}\leq n,\>i_{1}\neq j_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j},{\mathcal{I}}_{i_{1},j_{1}}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{i_{1},j_{1}},{\mathcal{I}}_{i_{2},j_{2}}\rangle_{l_{2}}

\displaystyle=(1-\delta_{j,n})\delta_{i_{2},n}\delta_{j_{2},1}+\delta_{j,n}\delta_{i_{2},n-1}\delta_{j_{2},1}.

Hence

\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{0}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{l_{2}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l r}0&\mathrm{if}\>k=1\\
\delta_{i,n-1}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{if}\>p=n,\>k>1\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{if}\>k>1\>\mathrm{and}\>p<n\end{array}\right.

Moreover according to Lemma
3.6.4.1
for any
1\leq i_{2},j_{2}\leq n
,
i_{2}\neq j_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i_{1},j_{1}\leq n,\>i_{1}\neq j_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j},{\mathcal{I}}_{i_{1},j_{1}}\rangle_{l_{2}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{i_{1},j_{1}},{\mathcal{I}}_{i_{2},j_{2}}\rangle_{l_{1}}=(1-\delta_{i,1})\delta_{i_{2},n}\delta_{j_{2},1}+\delta_{i,1}\delta_{i_{2},n}\delta_{j_{2},2}.

Hence

\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P},\>1\leq i,j\leq n,\>i\neq j}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{0}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{2}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{l_{1}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l r}0&\mathrm{if}\>k=1\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,2}&\mathrm{if}\>k=2\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{if}\>k>2\end{array}\right.

Finally according to Lemma
3.6.3.2
and Lemma
3.5.3.2
we have
\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{u},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}+l_{2}}=\chi\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]\cdot{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\right)
hence

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p},{\mathcal{I}}_{u}\rangle_{0}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{u},{\mathcal{I}}_{i,j}\rangle_{l_{1}+l_{2}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l r}0&\mathrm{if}\>k=1\\
\delta_{i,n}\delta_{j,1}&\mathrm{else}\end{array}\right.

We are now ready to compute
P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}(h_{1},W_{k,p})
. Moreover recall that the term of degree
(l_{1}+l_{2})
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is given by

\displaystyle P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}

\displaystyle(h_{1},v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}+l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w}-\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}}-\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l2}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{1}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{w,w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{0}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime\prime}}\rangle_{l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime\prime}}+\sum_{w,w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{0}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{2}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime\prime}}\rangle_{l_{1}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{0}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{1}+l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}}.

a)

If
v=w_{k,p}
, where
k\neq 1
.
Note that then the second and fourth terms of the sum are equal, and the third and fifth terms are equal, and the first and last terms are equal. We obtain

\displaystyle P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}(h_{1},w_{k,p})

\displaystyle=Q_{1}Q_{2}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}+{\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}+{\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}\right)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>k\neq 1.

b)

If
v=w_{1,p}
, where
p<n
.
Note that then all terms of the sum are equal to zero, except from the first term
\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}+l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w}
, which is non zero iff
w=w_{n,1}
, and
\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}}
, which is also non zero iff
w=w_{n,1}
. We obtain

\displaystyle P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}(h_{1},w_{1,p})=0

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>p<n

c)

if
v=w_{1,n}
.
Note that then
{\mathcal{O}}_{v}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{X(w_{1,n})}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{point}]
in
K(X)
. Then all terms of the sum are zero, except from the first term
\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}+l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w}
, which is non zero iff
w=w_{n,1}
, and
\sum_{w,w^{\prime}\in W^{P}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{v},{\mathcal{I}}_{w}\rangle_{l_{1}}\langle{\mathcal{O}}_{w},{\mathcal{I}}_{w^{\prime}}\rangle_{l_{2}}{\mathcal{O}}_{w^{\prime}}
, which is non zero iff
w=w_{n-1,1}
. We obtain

P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}(h_{1},w_{1,n})=Q_{1}Q_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{n,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1,1})=Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\right).

To sum up, we have

\displaystyle P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}(h_{1},w_{k,p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\mathrm{if}\>(k,p)\neq(1,n)\\
Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>(k,p)=(1,n)\end{array}\right.

Finally, by symmetry we deduce

\displaystyle P_{l_{1}+l_{2}}(h_{2},w_{k,p})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\mathrm{if}\>(k,p)\neq(1,n)\\
Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>(k,p)=(1,n)\end{array}\right.

3.7.4
Computing the Chevalley formula.

[Proof of Proposition
3.7.0.1
] According to Part
3.6
the only terms appearing in
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
are those of degree
l_{i}
and of degree
(l_{1}+l_{2})
. We now only have to sum the classical part
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
computed Proposition
3.4.1.1
with the part of degree
l_{1}
given by (
3.7.2
) and (
), the part of degree
l_{2}
given by (
) and (
3.7.1
), and finally the part of degree
(l_{1}+l_{2})
given by (
3.7.3
) and (
3.7.3
).

3.7.5
Comparison with small quantum cohomology.

Fulton and Woodward provide a Chevalley formula in in the small quantum cohomology ring of any generalized flag variety
[
FW04
]
. In the case of the incidence variety, this formula yields

\left\{\begin{aligned} h_{1}\star_{S}[X(i,j)]&=h_{1}\cdot[X(i,j)]+q_{1}[X]\delta_{i,2}\delta_{j,1}&\>\mathrm{if}\>i\neq 1;\\
h_{1}\star_{S}[X(1,j)]&=q_{1}[X(n,j)]&\>\mathrm{if}\>j<n;\\
h_{1}\star_{S}[X(1,n)]&=q_{1}[X(n-1,j)]+q_{1}q_{2}[X]&\>\mathrm{if}\>j=n,\end{aligned}\right.

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} h_{2}\star_{S}[X(i,j)]&=h_{2}\cdot[X(i,j)]+q_{2}[X]\delta_{n,i}\delta_{n-1,j}&\mathrm{si}\>j\neq n;\\
h_{2}\star_{S}[X(i,n)]&=q_{2}[X(i,1)]&\mathrm{si}\>i>1;\\
&=q_{2}[X(1,2)]+q_{1}q_{2}[X]&\mathrm{si}\>i=1,\end{aligned}\right.

where the product
\star_{S}
is the product in the small quantum cohomology ring of
X
.
If we compare the product in small quantum cohomology to the product in small quantum K theory, we observe (in blue the terms which are present in small quantum K-theory but not in small quantum cohomology) :

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1,n}+Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>k=1,p=n\\
Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n,p}&\mathrm{if}\>k=1,\>p<n\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,2}+Q_{1}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>k=2,p=1\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{k-1,p}&\mathrm{if}\>k>1,\>k\neq p+1\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{p-1,p}+{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{p-1,p+1}}&\mathrm{if}\>1<p<n-1,\>k=p+1\\
\end{array}\right.

3.7.6
Generalization to other adjoint varieties  ?

In
[
CP11b
]
Chaput-Perrin interpret Fulton and Woodward’s Chevalley formula in quantum cohomology in terms of roots systems. Note that their construction can be generalized to describe the Chevally formula in quantum
K
-theory described in Proposition
3.7.0.1
. One might wonder if this construction holds for other types of adjoint varieties.

3.8
On Littlewood-Richardson coefficients in
QK_{s}(X)
.

Recall that for any elements
u
and
v
in
W^{P}
there is a unique expression

{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}P_{u,v}^{w}(Q_{1},Q_{2}){\mathcal{O}}_{w}

where
P_{u,v}^{w}(Q_{1},Q_{2})
is a polynomial in the Novikov variables
Q_{1}
,
Q_{2}
. Note that taking the limit
Q_{1},Q_{2}\rightarrow 0
of
P_{u,v}^{w}(Q_{1},Q_{2})
yields the Littlewood-Richardson coefficients in
K(X)
.
We provide here an algorithm computing all coefficients
P_{u,v}^{w}(Q_{1},Q_{2})
, and provide a closed formula that matches our computations for small values of
n
. Furthermore, we prove that the signs of the coefficients of the polynomials
P_{u,v}^{w}(Q_{1},Q_{2})
satisfy a positivity rule.

3.8.1
An algorithm to compute Littlewood-Richardson coefficients in
QK_{s}(X)
.

Recall from Part
3.7

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1,n}+Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>k=1,p=n\\
Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n,p}&\mathrm{if}\>k=1,\>p<n\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,2}+Q_{1}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>k=2,p=1\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{k-1,p}&\mathrm{if}\>k>1,\>k\neq p+1\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{p-1,p}+{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}-{\mathcal{O}}_{p-1,p+1}&\mathrm{if}\>1<p<n-1,\>k=p+1\\
\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}Q_{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{1,2}+Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>k=1,p=n\\
Q_{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{k,1}&\mathrm{if}\>k>1,\>p=n\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1,n}+Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\right)&\mathrm{if}\>k=n,p=n-1\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p+1}&\mathrm{if}\>p<n\>k\neq p+1\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}+{\mathcal{O}}_{p-1,p}-{\mathcal{O}}_{p-1,p+1}&\mathrm{if}\>1<k<n-1,\>k=p+1\\
\end{array}\right.

This Chevalley formula allows us to write down an algorithm computing the product between any two Schubert classes in
QK(X)
(cf. appendix C for a python code implementing this algorithm) ; we indeed can compute for all
1\leq k,p\leq n
, where
k\neq p
,
\forall u\in W^{P}
, the product
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}
by following the here under steps :

—

For all
1<k\leq n
, compute
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}
iteratively by noticing
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,1}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{k+1,1}}
and initializing with
\mathcal{O}_{w_{n,1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}]\star\mathcal{O}_{u}=\mathcal{O}_{u}
;

—

for all
1\leq p<k\leq n
, compute
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}
iteratively by noticing
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{2}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p-1}}
;

—

compute
\mathcal{O}_{w_{1,2}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}
by noticing
\mathcal{O}_{w_{1,2}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{2,1}}-Q_{1}([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}})
;

—

for all
1\leq p<n
, compute
\mathcal{O}_{w_{p,p+1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}
iteratively by noticing
\mathcal{O}_{w_{p,p+1}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{p+1,p}}+(\mathcal{O}_{h_{2}}-id)\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{p-1,p}}
;

—

for all
1\leq k<p\leq n
, where
k\neq p-1
, compute
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u}
iteratively by noticing
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{k+1,p}}
.

3.8.2
Positivity in
QK_{s}(X)
.

Let
X\simeq G/P
be a generalized flag variety. For
u
,
v
in
W^{P}
decompose the quantum product
\mathcal{O}_{u}\star\mathcal{O}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P},\beta\in E(X)}N_{u,v}^{w,\beta}Q^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{w}
. For any elements
u
and
v
in
W^{P}
, we call the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}

positive
if for all
w\in W^{P}
,
\beta\in E(X)
, we have :

(-1)^{\mathrm{codim}(X(w))-\mathrm{codim}(X(u))-\mathrm{codim}(X(v))+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u,v}^{w,\beta}\geq 0.

(3.37)

Note that this is equivalent to

(-1)^{\mathrm{dim}(X(v))-\mathrm{codim}(X(u))-\mathrm{dim}(X(w))+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u,v}^{w,\beta}\geq 0.

Buch-Chaput-Mihalcea-Perrin proved that the product of any two Schubert classes is positive for
X
a cominuscule variety
[
BCMP16
]
. Denote by
\mathcal{O}_{u}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}N_{u,v}^{w}\mathcal{O}_{w}
the decomposition of the product of two Schubert classes in
K(X)
. Note that for
\beta=0
(
) yields

(-1)^{\mathrm{codim}(X(w))-\mathrm{codim}(X(u))-\mathrm{codim}(X(v))}N_{u,v}^{w}\geq 0.

Brion proved that, for
X
any generalized flag variety, the product of any two Schubert classes satisfies this positivity rule.

Let us go back now to our setting, i.e. let
X=Fl_{1,n-1}
.

Proposition 3.8.2.1
.

Let
u
,
v
be elements in
W^{P}
. The product
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

Let
u=w_{k,p}
in
W^{P}
. We will prove here that the product of
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}
with any Schubert class is positive by induction on
(k,p)
, running over the steps of the algorithm described here above.
We will use the following formulas. According to (
3.3
)

X(i,j)=\left\{\left([x_{1}:\dots:x_{i}:0:\dots:0],[0:\dots 0:y_{j}:\dots:y_{n}]\right)\in\mathbb{P}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}\right\},

we have

{\rm dim}X(k,p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}k+n-p-1&\mathrm{if}\>k<p\\
k+n-p-2&\mathrm{if}\>k>p\end{array}\right.=(k+n-p-2)+\Gamma_{p-k,0}

where we denote by
\Gamma_{n,0}
the symbol of value
1
if
n\geq 0
,
0
else. Furthermore note that since
X
is a degree
(1,1)
hypersurface of
{\mathbb{P}}^{n-1}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{n-1}
the adjunction formula yields for any
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 0

\int_{d_{1}l_{1}+d_{2}l_{2}}c_{1}(T_{X})=d_{1}(n-1)+d_{2}(n-1)

Let us our now begin our induction.

—

Let
1<k\leq n
.
Then
{\mathcal{O}}_{k,1}={\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{k+1,1}
. Suppose the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{k+1,1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive for all
v\in W^{P}
. Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. According to Lemma
3.8.2.1

i)

{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive, Hence according to Lemma
3.8.2.1

iii)

{\mathcal{O}}_{k,1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive. Indeed, for
k>1
we have
{\rm dim}X(k,1)=k+n-3={\rm dim}X(k+1,1)-1
.

—

Let
1\leq p<k\leq n
.
Suppose the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p-1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive for all
v\in W^{P}
. Note that
\mathcal{O}_{{k,p}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{2}}\star\mathcal{O}_{{k,p-1}}
. Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. According to Lemma
3.8.2.1

i)

{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive, Hence according to Lemma
3.8.2.1

iii)

{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive. Indeed, for
1\leq p<k\leq n
we have
p-1<k
and
{\rm dim}X(k,p)=k+n-p-1={\rm dim}X(k,p-1)-1
.

—

For
k=1
and
p=2
.
Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. Note that
\mathcal{O}_{w_{1,2}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{2,1}}-Q_{1}([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}})
. Since
{\rm dim}X(1,2)=n-2={\rm dim}X(2,1)-1
, according to Lemma
3.8.2.1

ii)
and
iii)
the term
mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{2,1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
has the expected sign. Furthermore, since
{\rm dim}X-1-(n-1)=2n-4-n+1={\rm dim}X(1,2)
, according to Lemma
3.8.2.1

iv)
, the terms
-Q_{1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
and
Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
have the expected sign.
Hence the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive for all
w\in W^{P}
.

—

Let
1\leq p<n
.
Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. Suppose the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{p+1,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive. Note that
\mathcal{O}_{w_{p,p+1}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{p+1,p}}+(\mathcal{O}_{h_{2}}-id)\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{p-1,p}}
. Note that
{\rm dim}X(p,p+1)=n-2={\rm dim}X(p+1,p)-1
. Hence, since
{\mathcal{O}}_{p+1,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive
-{\mathcal{O}}_{p+1,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
has the same sign as the expected sign for
{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
. Furthermore, according to Lemma
3.8.2.1
both
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
have the same sign as the expected sign for
{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
. Hence
{\mathcal{O}}_{p,p+1}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

—

Let
1\leq k<p\leq n
, where
k\neq p-1
.
Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. Suppose the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{k+1,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive. Note that
\mathcal{O}_{w_{k,p}}=\mathcal{O}_{h_{1}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w_{k+1,p}}
.Since
k<p-1
,
{\rm dim}X(k,p)=k+n-p-1={\rm dim}X(k+1,p)
. Hence according to Lemma
3.8.2.1

iii)
the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

Finally by induction
{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive for all
v
in
W^{P}
.

Lemma 3.8.2.1
.

i)

For any element
v
in
W^{P}
the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

ii)

For any element
v
in
W^{P}
the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

iii)

Let
u
be an element in
W^{P}
. Suppose there exists an element
u^{\prime}
in
W^{P}
such that
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}={\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}
, and
{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive for all
v
in
W^{P}
. Furthermore suppose
{\rm dim}X(u)={\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-1
. Then for any element
v
in
W^{P}
the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

iv)

Let
u
be an element in
W^{P}
. Suppose there exists an element
u^{\prime}
in
W^{P}
such that
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}=Q_{j}{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}
, where
j\in\{1,2\}
, and
{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive for all
v
in
W^{P}
. Furthermore suppose
{\rm dim}X(u)={\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-(n-1)
. Then for any element
v
in
W^{P}
the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive.

Let
1\leq k,p\leq n
.

Démonstration.

i)

We compute explicitly
(-1)^{\mathrm{dim}(X(v))-\mathrm{codim}(h_{i})-\mathrm{dim}(X(w))+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u,v}^{w,\beta}
using (
3.7.0.1
). Note that according to
[
Bri02
]

N_{u,v}^{w,0}
has the expected sign, hence we only have to consider terms
N_{u,v}^{w,\beta}
with
\beta>0
.

(a)

Recall we have
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{1,n}=Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1,n}+Q_{1}Q_{2}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\right)
. We have

(-1)^{dimX(n-1,n)-{\rm dim}X(1,n)-{\rm codim}h_{1}+(n-1)}=(-1)^{(n-1)-0-1+(n-2)+(n-1)}=(-1)^{2n-4}\geq 0.

(-1)^{{\rm dim}X-{\rm dim}X(1,n)-{\rm codim}h_{1}+2(n-1)}=(-1)^{2n-3-1}\geq 0.

Hence
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{1,n}
is positive.

(b)

Let
1<p<n
. Recall we have
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{1,p}=Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n,p}
. We have

(-1)^{dimX(1,p)-{\rm dim}X(n,p)-{\rm codim}h_{i}+(n-1)}=(-1)^{(n-p-1)-(2n-p-2)-1+(n-1)}\geq 0.

(c)

Recall we have
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{2,1}={\mathcal{O}}_{1,2}+Q_{1}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]-{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\right)
. We have

(-1)^{dimX-{\rm dim}X(2,1)-{\rm codim}h_{i}+(n-1)}=(-1)^{(2n-3)-(n-2)-1+(n-1)}\geq 0.

ii)

Note that by symmetry if there is a term in
Q_{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j}
, we have a term in
Q_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{n-p+1,n-k+1}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{n-i+1,n-j+1}
, and if there is a term in
Q_{1}Q_{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{k,p}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j}
, we have a term in
Q_{1}Q_{2}{\mathcal{O}}_{n-p+1,n-k+1}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{1}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{n-i+1,n-j+1}
. Since

\displaystyle{\rm dim}X(n-j+1,n-i+1)

\displaystyle=n-j+1+n-(n-i+1)-1+\Gamma_{n-i+1-(n-j+1),0}

\displaystyle=n+i-j-1+\Gamma_{j-i,0}

\displaystyle={\rm dim}X(i,j),

part
i)
yields the positivity of
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{2}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
for all
v
in
W^{P}
.

iii)

Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. Consider the decomposition
\mathcal{O}_{u^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{O}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P},\beta\in E(X)}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta}Q^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{w}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
in the Schubert basis. Since the quantum product
\star
is associative we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\star\mathcal{O}_{u^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{O}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P},\beta_{1}\in E(X)}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta}Q^{\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\star\mathcal{O}_{w}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w,\>w^{\prime}\in W^{P},\>\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in E(X)}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta_{1}}Q^{\beta_{1}}N_{h_{i},w}^{w^{\prime},\beta_{2}}Q^{\beta_{2}}\mathcal{O}_{w^{\prime}}

hence
N_{u,v}^{w^{\prime},\beta}=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1},\>\beta_{2}\in E(X),\\
\>\beta=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\end{subarray}}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta_{1}}N_{h_{i},w}^{w^{\prime},\beta_{2}}
. Since
{\rm dim}X(u)={\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-1
and the products
{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{h_{i}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{w}
are positive we have

\displaystyle(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(u)-{\rm dim}X(w^{\prime})-{\rm codim}X(v)+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}

\displaystyle N_{u,v}^{w^{\prime},\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-1-{\rm dim}X(w^{\prime})-{\rm codim}X(v)+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta_{1}}N_{h_{i},w}^{w^{\prime},\beta_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-{\rm dim}X(w)-{\rm codim}X(v)+\int_{\beta_{1}}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta_{1}}

\displaystyle\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\times(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(w)-{\rm dim}X(w^{\prime})-{\rm codim}h_{i}+\int_{\beta_{2}}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{h_{i},w}^{w^{\prime},\beta_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq 0

iv)

Let
v
be an element in
W^{P}
. Consider the decomposition
\mathcal{O}_{u^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{O}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P},\beta\in E(X)}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta}Q^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{w}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
in the Schubert basis. We have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{u}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}

\displaystyle=Q_{j}\mathcal{O}_{u^{\prime}}\star\mathcal{O}_{v}=\sum_{w\in W^{P},\beta\in E(X)}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta+l_{j}}Q^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{w}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{P},\>\beta\in E(X)}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta+l_{j}}Q^{\beta}

hence
N_{u,v}^{w^{\prime},\beta}=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in E(X),\>\beta\geq l_{j}\end{subarray}}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta-l_{j}}
. Since
{\rm dim}X(u)={\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-(n-1)
and the product
{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}\star{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
is positive we have

\displaystyle(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(u)-{\rm dim}X(w^{\prime})-{\rm codim}X(v)+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}

\displaystyle N_{u,v}^{w^{\prime},\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-(n-1)-{\rm dim}X(w^{\prime})-{\rm codim}X(v)+\int_{\beta}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta-l_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{P}}(-1)^{{\rm dim}X(u^{\prime})-{\rm dim}X(w)-{\rm codim}X(v)+\int_{\beta-l_{j}}c_{1}(T_{X})}N_{u^{\prime},v}^{w,\beta-l_{j}}

\displaystyle\geq 0

∎

3.8.3
A conjectural formula for Littlewood-Richardson coefficients in
QK_{s}(X)
.

We computed for various values of
n
Littlewood-Richardson coefficients in
QK_{s}(X)
using the code in Appendix B. The results allowed us to guess and write down the following formula. This formula has been verified for small values of
n
.
Let us now introduce some specific notations we need to express our result. First, we define "degree operators"
d_{i}
on
(W^{P})^{3}
by :

\displaystyle\forall 1\leq i,j,k,p,s,t\leq n,\>i\neq j,\>k\neq p,\>s\neq t,\>\>\>\>

\displaystyle d_{1}(w_{i,j},w_{k,p},w_{s,t})=1-\lfloor\frac{i+k-s}{n}\rfloor;

\displaystyle d_{2}(w_{i,j},w_{k,p},w_{s,t})=\lfloor\frac{j+p-t}{n}\rfloor,

where
\lfloor x\rfloor
is the integer part of
x
.
Furthermore, let us define maps
t_{i}:(W^{P})^{2}\rightarrow W^{P}
by :

\displaystyle\forall 1\leq i,j,k\leq n,\>i\neq j,\>k\neq p,\>\>\>\>

\displaystyle t_{0}(w_{i,j},w_{k,p})=w_{(i+k-1)mod(n)+1,(j+p-2)mod(n)+1}

\displaystyle t_{1}(w_{i,j},w_{k,p})=w_{(i+k-2)mod(n)+1,(j+p-2)mod(n)+1}

\displaystyle t_{2}(w_{i,j},w_{k,p})=w_{(i+k-1)mod(n)+1,(j+p-1)mod(n)+1}

\displaystyle t_{3}(w_{i,j},w_{k,p})=w_{(i+k-2)mod(n)+1,(j+p-1)mod(n)+1}.

Finally, we define an operator
\Delta
on
(W^{P})^{3}
by setting :

\displaystyle\forall u,\>v,\>w\>\in W^{P},\>\Delta(u,v,w)

\displaystyle=1\>

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>(-1)^{\ell(w_{0}w)-\ell(w_{0}u)-\ell(w_{0}v)+\int_{d_{1}(u,v,w)l_{1}+d_{2}(u,v,w)l_{2}}c_{1}(T_{X})}\geq 0

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathrm{if}\>(-1)^{\ell(w_{0}w)-\ell(w_{0}u)-\ell(w_{0}v)+\int_{d_{1}(u,v,w)l_{1}+d_{2}(u,v,w)l_{2}}c_{1}(T_{X})}<0

Conjecture 3.8.3.1
.

The product between two Schubert classes in
QK_{s}(X)
is given by the following formula :

\displaystyle\forall\>u,\>v\in W^{P},\>\>\mathcal{O}_{u}\star\mathcal{O}_{v}=

\displaystyle\>\>\>\>\>\>\Delta(u,v,t_{0}(u,v))

\displaystyle q_{1}^{d_{1}(u,v,t_{0}(u,v))}q_{2}^{d_{2}(u,v,t_{0}(u,v))}\mathcal{O}_{t_{0}(u,v)}

\displaystyle+(1-\Delta(u,v,t_{1}(u,v)))\bigg(

\displaystyle q_{1}^{d_{1}(u,v,t_{1}(u,v))}q_{2}^{d_{2}(u,v,t_{1}(u,v))}\mathcal{O}_{t_{1}(u,v)}\>\>

\displaystyle+q_{1}^{d_{1}(u,v,t_{2}(u,v))}q_{2}^{d_{2}(u,v,t_{2}(u,v))}\mathcal{O}_{t_{2}(u,v)}\>\>

\displaystyle-q_{1}^{d_{1}(u,v,t_{3}(u,v))}q_{2}^{d_{2}(u,v,t_{3}(u,v))}\mathcal{O}_{t_{3}(u,v)}\bigg)

.
